### عَنِ ٱلْيَمِينِ — the disagreement over it

**And this expression is one of the most disputed places in the sūra.**

***Yamīn* — root ي-م-ن.** The root has three separate branches in Arabic, and all are used in the Qurʾān:

| Branch | Its sense | Example in the Qurʾān |
|---|---|---|
| **The right side / the right hand** | Direction and limb | *aṣḥābu'l-yamīn* (al-Wāqiʿa 56/27) |
| **An oath** | From the use of the right hand in clasping | *aymānukum* (al-Māʾida 5/89) |
| **Power / strength** | From the right hand's being the strong hand | *la-akhadhnā minhu bi'l-**yamīn*** (al-Ḥāqqa 69/45) |
| **Blessing / good fortune** | *Yumn* — blessing, good fortune | *al-ayman* (al-Qaṣaṣ 28/30 — *al-wādi'l-ayman*) |

**And on the sense of the expression here there is more than one view in the classical commentaries. I give them in a table and impose no preference:**

| View | What *ʿani'l-yamīn* means | Ground | Its weak side |
|---|---|---|---|
| **From the side of religion / in the guise of good** | "You used to come at us in the dress of piety" — offering evil as good | *Yamīn* in Arabic announces the side of good fortune and good; the verse in which the satan comes *"from before them, from behind them, from their right, from their left"* (al-Aʿrāf 7/17) supports this reading | The verse says not *bi'l-yamīn* but *ʿani'l-yamīn*; the difference of preposition requires explanation |
| **By force / by compulsion** | "You used to come at us by establishing dominance" | *Yamīn* is also used in the sense of power (al-Ḥāqqa 69/45) | The next verse rejects this: *mā kāna lanā ʿalaykum min sulṭān* (30) |
| **With oaths / by swearing** | "You would come swearing oaths and giving assurance" | *Yamīn* = an oath; the theme of deceiving by oath is present in the Qurʾān (al-Qalam 68/10 ff.) | Because the word comes singular and definite, it is strained toward the sense of "oaths" |
| **From the strongest / most trusted side** | "You would approach us from the safest side" | In Arabic the right side is the side of trust and of strength | It overlaps with those above and may not count as a separate view |

**The weakness of the second view is read off the text, and this must be recorded:** in the thirtieth verse the other side rejects the claim of power by saying *"we had no *sulṭān* over you"*. **The text, that is, produces within itself an objection to this reading.** This is not a preference; it is a datum of the text.

**As my own reading — and it is not binding:** the preposition in the expression *ʿani'l-yamīn* (*ʿan*, "from, from the side of") announces a **direction**, and this gives a ground to the first view. "To come from the right" is the picture of an enemy approaching from the unexpected and unguarded side. **But I do not set this down as a ruling;** the word is open to more than one branch and the text does not specify.

### Who is speaking to whom?

**The verse names neither side.** There is only *qālū … qālū*.

**In the classical commentaries two divisions are transmitted:**

| Division | Who says 28 | Who says 29-32 |
|---|---|---|
| **Followers / leaders** | Those who followed | The leaders, those who grew proud |
| **Human beings / satans** | Human beings | The satans / *qarīn*s who fastened on them |

**Both are transmitted and the text is open to both.** The expression *fa-aghwaynākum* ("we led you astray") in the thirty-second verse suits the second reading, while *mā kāna lanā ʿalaykum min sulṭān* in the thirtieth suits both. **I do not impose a preference.**

### A comparison with Ghāfir 40/47-50

**The scene of the quarrel in the fire was treated in detail at `040-gafir.md` 40/47-50. The three-stage chain table there is not repeated; here I set down a comparison of the two scenes.**

| | **Ghāfir 40/47-50** | **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/27-32** |
|---|---|---|
| **The verb** | *yataḥājjūna fi'n-nār* (ح-ج-ج) — **to argue with proofs** | *yatasāʾalūn* (س-أ-ل) — **to question** |
| **The parties** | ***al-ḍuʿafāʾ*** / ***alladhīna'stakbarū*** — plainly named | **Unnamed** — only *baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍ* |
| **The first word** | *innā kunnā lakum **tabaʿā*** — "we followed you" | *innakum kuntum taʾtūnanā **ʿani'l-yamīn*** — "you used to come at us from the right" |
| **The demand** | **The sharing of the burden** — *fa-hal antum mughnūna ʿannā naṣīban mina'n-nār* | **No demand** — only accusation |
| **The answer** | *innā kullun fīhā* — "we are all in it" | *bal lam takūnū muʾminīn* — "you were not believers to begin with" |
| **A third stage** | **Present** — appeal to the keepers (49-50) | **Absent** — the quarrel stays between two parties |
| **The closing sentence** | *wa mā duʿāʾu'l-kāfirīna illā fī ḍalāl* | *fa-innahum yawmaʾidhin fi'l-ʿadhābi mushtarikūn* (33) |

**The most marked difference between the two scenes is this, and it is read off the text:** in Ghāfir the weak make a **request** (share the burden); in al-Ṣāffāt there is no request at all, only **accusation**. **In Ghāfir the quarrel is looking for a solution; in al-Ṣāffāt a settling of scores is going on.**

**The second difference:** Ghāfir **gives names** to the parties (the weak / those who grew proud); al-Ṣāffāt does not. **The result of this is that the scene in al-Ṣāffāt turns into a quarrel whose parties are undefined** — and, as was recorded above, in the classical commentaries this indefiniteness has led to two different divisions.

**I record this as my own reading:** in Ghāfir the quarrel has an **addressee** (the keepers), in al-Ṣāffāt it has none. This is why the scene in al-Ṣāffāt seems to take place in a closed room: no one calls out, everyone turns to the one beside him. **The question asked in the twenty-fifth verse (*"why do you not help one another?"*) is answered by this scene:** in place of help they produce accusation.

### مِّن سُلْطَٰن — a power

**Root س-ل-ط: to gain mastery over something, to dominate it.** *Sulṭān* — a proof, an argument; and the power of rule. The root was treated at `040-gafir.md` 40/56 (*bi-ghayri sulṭānin atāhum*) and at `053-necm.md` 53/23 (*mā anzala'llāhu bihā min sulṭān*).

**A point the lexicographers record:** *sulṭān* is connected with *salīṭ* (oil, olive oil) from the same root — **as the oil takes up the wick and keeps the light standing, so a proof keeps a claim standing.** I give this link as a transmission from the lexicographers.

***Min* here comes as *zāʾida* (a reinforcing particle):** *mā kāna lanā … min sulṭān* — "we had no power or proof whatever". It generalises the negation.

### فَحَقَّ عَلَيْنَا قَوْلُ رَبِّنَآ — and the admission of guilt

**Root ح-ق-ق: a thing's being fixed and in place.** *Ḥaqqa ʿalayhi* — it came true against him, it became unavoidable. The root was treated in `050-kaf.md`.

***Qawlu rabbinā* — "the word of our Lord".** Which word is not said here; elsewhere the Qurʾān names the word about the filling of the fire (al-Sajda 32/13; Hūd 11/119). **I give no name; the verse gives none.**

### فَأَغْوَيْنَٰكُمْ إِنَّا كُنَّا غَٰوِينَ — and the form of the confession

**Root غ-و-ي: to go from the way, to lose one's bearings, to miss the mark.** *Ghayy* — waywardness; *ghāwī* — one who goes astray.

**And the structure of the sentence is worth noting:**

- *fa-aghwaynākum* — Form IV, **transitive**: "we led you astray".
- *innā kunnā **ghāwīn*** — an active participle from Form I, **intransitive**: "we ourselves had gone astray".

**The confession thus has two layers:** they admit the act they did, but they give as its ground **their own condition.** "We led you astray — because we had already gone astray."

**I record this as my own reading:** this is not a defence but an **equalising**. The other side was saying "you led me astray"; the answer becomes "yes, but we too had gone astray." **The guilt is admitted, that is, but the difference is abolished.** And the next verse seals it: *fa-innahum yawmaʾidhin fi'l-ʿadhābi **mushtarikūn***.

---

## 37/33-39 — فَإِنَّهُمْ يَوْمَئِذٍ فِى ٱلْعَذَابِ مُشْتَرِكُونَ · إِنَّا كَذَٰلِكَ نَفْعَلُ بِٱلْمُجْرِمِينَ · إِنَّهُمْ كَانُوٓا۟ إِذَا قِيلَ لَهُمْ لَآ إِلَٰهَ إِلَّا ٱللَّهُ يَسْتَكْبِرُونَ · وَيَقُولُونَ أَئِنَّا لَتَارِكُوٓا۟ ءَالِهَتِنَا لِشَاعِرٍ مَّجْنُونٍۭ · بَلْ جَآءَ بِٱلْحَقِّ وَصَدَّقَ ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ · إِنَّكُمْ لَذَآئِقُوا۟ ٱلْعَذَابِ ٱلْأَلِيمِ · وَمَا تُجْزَوْنَ إِلَّا مَا كُنتُمْ تَعْمَلُونَ

*Fa-innahum yawmaʾidhin fi'l-ʿadhābi mushtarikūn · Innā kadhālika nafʿalu bi'l-mujrimīn · Innahum kānū idhā qīla lahum lā ilāha illa'llāhu yastakbirūn · Wa yaqūlūna a-innā la-tārikū ālihatinā li-shāʿirin majnūn · Bal jāʾa bi'l-ḥaqqi wa ṣaddaqa'l-mursalīn · Innakum la-dhāʾiqu'l-ʿadhābi'l-alīm · Wa mā tujzawna illā mā kuntum taʿmalūn*

"That day they are partners in the punishment. Thus do We deal with the guilty. For when it was said to them 'there is no god but God' they would grow proud, and would say: 'Are we to leave our gods for a mad poet?' — No! He brought the truth and confirmed the messengers who were sent. — You will surely taste the painful punishment. And you are requited only for what you used to do."

### مُشْتَرِكُونَ — partnership

**Root ش-ر-ك, Form VIII: to be a partner.** And the choice of the word is sharp.

**The same root is the root of the word *shirk*** — to set partners beside God. **Here the sūra uses the same root for their punishment: those who set partners become partners in the punishment.**

**In Arabic rhetoric this is close to the method called *mushākala* (using the same word as the response).** What is recorded is this: the act and its requital are chosen from the same root.

**And its link with the thirty-second verse is plain:** a moment before, the parties were throwing the guilt at one another. **The verse closes the matter: there is no separating, there is partnership.**

### إِنَّا كَذَٰلِكَ نَفْعَلُ بِٱلْمُجْرِمِينَ — and the first form of the mould

**And this sentence is the *reverse* of the closing mould in the sūra's sequence of prophets.**

| Verse | Sentence | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **34** | *innā **kadhālika nafʿalu bi'l-mujrimīn*** | The guilty |
| **80, 105, 121, 131** | *innā **kadhālika najzi'l-muḥsinīn*** | Noah, Abraham, Moses-Aaron, Elijah |
| **110** | ***kadhālika najzi'l-muḥsinīn*** | Abraham |

**The same skeleton of a mould — *innā kadhālika* + verb + *bi*/*li* + a group — is applied to two opposite groups. This is a fact countable from the text.**

**And the difference between the two verbs is worth noting:**

- *nafʿalu **bi**'l-mujrimīn* — "we **do** to the guilty". The verb is *faʿl* (to do), the preposition *bāʾ*.
- *najzi'l-muḥsinīn* — "we **requite** those who do good". The verb is *jazāʾ* (to give a requital), directly transitive.

**I record this as my own reading:** the word *jazāʾ* announces an **equivalence** — a requital equal to what was done. *Faʿl* announces only the act. **On the side of good, then, the language of reciprocity is used; on the side of guilt, the language of procedure.** This is an observation about word choice.

***Mujrim* — root ج-ر-م.** The lexicographers give the original picture of the root as **"to pluck fruit from the branch / to cut"**: *jarama'n-nakhla* — he harvested the palm. **From here the sense "to earn" and then "evil earning, guilt" arises.** *Jurm* — a crime; *mujrim* — one who commits it.

### إِذَا قِيلَ لَهُمْ لَآ إِلَٰهَ إِلَّا ٱللَّهُ يَسْتَكْبِرُونَ — the cause

**The thirty-fifth verse gives the ground of the thirty-fourth: why it is done thus.**

**And the ground is the reaction they showed to a single sentence:** *lā ilāha illa'llāh.*

***Yastakbirūn* — root ك-ب-ر, Form X.** The work of the pattern: **to see oneself as great, to give oneself airs of greatness.** The root was treated in `040-gafir.md` as the backbone of that sūra (*al-kibr*, *yastakbirūn*).

**A point of word order:** the sentence is built as *innahum **kānū** … yastakbirūn* — *kāna* + imperfect. **In Arabic this announces a habit continuing in the past:** not once, but **every time.**

**And the conditional particle is *idhā* (whenever), not *in* (if).** The difference between the two: *idhā* is used for what is certain to occur, *in* for what is possible. **Not "when it was said to them", then, but "every time it was said to them".**

### أَئِنَّا لَتَارِكُوٓا۟ ءَالِهَتِنَا لِشَاعِرٍ مَّجْنُونٍ — the accusation

**Two accusations together: *shāʿir* and *majnūn*.**

***Shāʿir* — root ش-ع-ر: to perceive finely, to sense.** *Shuʿūr* — becoming aware; *shiʿr* — poetry. **The link the lexicographers make:** a poet is one who perceives what others do not and says it in measured speech. In the root of the word there is **fineness and perception**, not **fabrication**.

**This accusation occurs in several places in the Qurʾān and was treated at `069-hakka.md` 69/41** (*wa mā huwa bi-qawli shāʿirin qalīlan mā tuʾminūn*) **and at `052-tur.md` 52/30** (*am yaqūlūna shāʿirun natarabbaṣu bihi rayba'l-manūn*). **I rely on those treatments.**

***Majnūn* — root ج-ن-ن: to cover, to hide.** *Jinn* — the unseen; *janīn* — the covered one in the womb; *janna* — a garden covered with trees; *majnūn* — one whose reason is covered. **The root was analysed in `072-cin.md`.**

**And the oddity of the two accusations coming together must be recorded — as my own reading:** to be a *shāʿir* requires **skill**; to be *majnūn* announces **the absence of skill**. **The two accusations refute one another.** This pair of accusations occurs together in several places in the Qurʾān, and a similar pair was treated at `051-zariyat.md` 51/52 (*sāḥirun aw majnūn*).

**A point of word order:** *li-shāʿirin* — the preposition *lām* announces **cause**: "**because of** a poet". What is named, that is, is not what they would gain in return for leaving their gods but **the person** who leads them to leave them. **The expression reduces the matter to an individual.**

And *ālihati**nā*** — "**our** gods". In the fourth verse it had been said *inna ilāha**kum** la-wāḥid* — "**your** God is one". **The same word, the same pronominal suffix, the opposite ruling.** The sūra sets its own key word in their mouths. This is a textual datum.

### بَلْ جَآءَ بِٱلْحَقِّ وَصَدَّقَ ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ — the answer

***Bal*** is again *iḍrāb*: it leaves the accusation and announces the truth.

**And the answer is two sentences — and both are answers to the accusation:**

| Accusation | Answer | How it meets it |
|---|---|---|
| ***Shāʿir*** — he brings invented speech | *jāʾa **bi'l-ḥaqq*** — he brought the truth | The quality of what he brought |
| ***Majnūn*** — he speaks on his own | *wa **ṣaddaqa'l-mursalīn*** — he confirmed the messengers | The accord of what he brought with those before him |

**The second answer is worth noting:** what is shown as proof is not something new but **continuity.** "This man is not alone; he says the same thing as those before him." **One who is mad is not consistent.**

***Al-mursalīn* — root ر-س-ل.** **And this word will occur six times in the sūra:** 37 (here), 123, 133, 139, 171, 181. **The three introductory moulds in the sequence of prophets (Elijah, Lot, Jonah) use this word, and the sūra's closing with peace (181) uses the same word.** This is a countable structural datum.

**I record this as my own reading:** that it is said in the thirty-seventh verse *"he confirmed the messengers"* announces in advance the sūra's later sequence of prophets. **Who the confirmed messengers are will be enumerated one by one from the seventy-fifth verse.** This link between the two blocks is not set up openly in the text; the sharing of the word is a textual datum.

### إِنَّكُمْ لَذَآئِقُوا۟ ٱلْعَذَابِ ٱلْأَلِيمِ — the address turns

**And here there is *iltifāt* (a shift in the direction of address):** in verses thirty-three to thirty-seven the third person (*innahum*, *yaqūlūn*); in the thirty-eighth **the second person** (*innakum*).

**The same transition was treated at `056-vakia.md` 56/51**, and what was recorded there was this: **the telling turns to the addressee at the point where it becomes most concrete.** The same observation is at work here.

***Dhāʾiqū* — root ذ-و-ق: to taste.** And in the thirty-first verse the same word had come from the mouth of the other side: *innā la-**dhāʾiqūn*** ("we too shall taste it"). **The same word two verses apart, one about themselves and one about them.** This is a textual datum.

**The choice of the verb:** *to taste* announces that the punishment is **direct and personal.** Not hearing or seeing; contact with the mouth.

### وَمَا تُجْزَوْنَ إِلَّا مَا كُنتُمْ تَعْمَلُونَ — the measure

**The block closes with a sentence of measure: *only the requital of what you did*.**

***Tujzawn* — root ج-ز-ي: to requite, to pay what is equal.** In the thirty-fourth verse *nafʿalu* was used; here *tujzawn.* **The block closes, that is, by passing from the language of "procedure" to the language of "requital".**

**The restriction set up with *illā*** announces that there is **no excess** in the punishment. **This is a measure repeated in the Qurʾān, and similar sentences were treated in the sections `045-casiye.md` and `046-ahkaf.md`.**

**And the next verse will give the other face of this measure:** *illā ʿibāda'llāhi'l-mukhlaṣīn* (40) — the exception. **The punishment is exactly equivalent; but there is a group exempted from the equivalence.**

---

## 37/40-44 — إِلَّا عِبَادَ ٱللَّهِ ٱلْمُخْلَصِينَ · أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ لَهُمْ رِزْقٌ مَّعْلُومٌ · فَوَٰكِهُ ۖ وَهُم مُّكْرَمُونَ · فِى جَنَّٰتِ ٱلنَّعِيمِ · عَلَىٰ سُرُرٍ مُّتَقَٰبِلِينَ

*Illā ʿibāda'llāhi'l-mukhlaṣīn · Ulāʾika lahum rizqun maʿlūm · Fawākihu wa hum mukramūn · Fī jannāti'n-naʿīm · ʿAlā sururin mutaqābilīn*

"Except God's chosen servants. For them there is a known provision: fruits. And they will be honoured — in gardens of bliss, upon couches facing one another."

### إِلَّا عِبَادَ ٱللَّهِ ٱلْمُخْلَصِينَ — the refrain of the sūra

**This sentence occurs four times in the sūra: 40, 74, 128, 160. This is a countable fact, and it forms the structural skeleton of the sūra.**

| Verse | Exception to what |
|---|---|
| **40** | Those who will taste the punishment (38-39) |
| **74** | The evil end of those who were warned (73) |
| **128** | Those who denied Elijah (127) |
| **160** | Those who ascribe things to God (159) |

**In all four the same work is done: a group is set apart from a ruling.** And in all four places the ruling is negative and the exception positive.

**And there is a fifth occurrence — but this time the sentence is inside a falsehood:**

| Verse | Expression | Who says it |
|---|---|---|
| **169** | *la-kunnā **ʿibāda'llāhi'l-mukhlaṣīn*** | **From the mouths of the deniers:** "we would surely have been God's chosen servants" |

**The sūra, that is, gives the very construction it set down four times as the condition of deliverance a fifth time as an unrealised claim of the deniers.** And immediately after: *fa-kafarū bihi* — "then they denied it" (170). **This is a structure that can be verified from the text and is one of the sūra's most elegant internal links.**

### ٱلْمُخْلَصِين — variant reading and sense

**Root خ-ل-ص: a thing's being pure, unmixed.** The root was analysed at `002-bakara.md` 2/139 (*wa naḥnu lahu mukhliṣūn*): **like the separating of butter from milk, a thing's having nothing else mixed into it.** I do not repeat the definition given there.

**But here there is a difference of pattern, and it changes the sense:**

| Reading | Pattern | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| **ٱلْمُخْلَصِين** (*mukhlaṣīn*) | **Passive participle** — Form IV passive | **Chosen, made pure** — the doer is God |
| **ٱلْمُخْلِصِين** (*mukhliṣīn*) | **Active participle** — Form IV active | **Sincere, single-hearted** — the doer is the servant |

**Both readings are transmitted in the sources on the variant readings. I give no reciter's name.**

**The difference of sense is marked and must be recorded:** on the first reading the source of deliverance is **being chosen**, on the second **sincerity**. **And the two readings do not exclude one another** — both patterns are used in the Qurʾān: *innahu min ʿibādina'l-**mukhlaṣīn*** (Yūsuf 12/24) and *mukhliṣīna lahu'd-dīn* (al-Bayyina 98/5; al-Zumar 39/2).

**I do not impose a preference.** In this commentary the form in the received orthography of the text (*mukhlaṣīn*) has been taken as the basis and rendered "chosen"; that the other is also transmitted has been recorded.

***ʿIbād* — root ع-ب-د.** The word will occur in the sūra's closing mould as well: *innahu min **ʿibādinā**'l-muʾminīn* (81, 111, 132). **Note: there the construct is *ʿibādinā* (Our servants), here *ʿibāda'llāh* (God's servants).** The two constructs say the same thing, but one in the first person and the other in the third. **This is an observation about the structure.**

### رِزْقٌ مَّعْلُومٌ — a known provision

**Root ر-ز-ق: to convey to a living being what it will benefit from.** The root was treated at `051-zariyat.md` 51/22 (*wa fi's-samāʾi rizqukum*) and 51/58 (*inna'llāha huwa'r-razzāq*).

**مَعْلُوم — root ع-ل-م, a passive participle: known, determined.**

**And the choice of the attribute has been dwelt upon.** The explanations transmitted in the classical commentaries:

| Explanation | What it says |
|---|---|
| **Its time is known** | Uninterrupted and regular; no uncertainty |
| **Its quality is known** | What it is, is known; there is no surprise |
| **Known to its owner** | Determined and measured with God |

**All three are transmitted; the verse does not specify and I impose no preference.**

**But a contrast must be recorded — and this is a textual datum:** in the section on al-Wāqiʿa it was said of *zaqqūm* that it is *"a tree of unknown, unrecognised kind"*, and the **estranging** function of that indefiniteness was given there in a table (`056-vakia.md`). **Here the reverse is present: *maʿlūm* — known.** And in the sixty-second verse the sūra will compare these two sides directly: *a-dhālika khayrun nuzulan am shajaratu'z-zaqqūm.*

| Side | Attribute | Its effect |
|---|---|---|
| **The mukhlaṣīn (41)** | *rizqun **maʿlūm*** | **Familiar, determined** |
| **The wrongdoers (64)** | *shajaratun **takhruju fī aṣli'l-jaḥīm*** | **Alien, indescribable** |

**And the word will occur once more in the sūra:** *wa mā minnā illā lahu maqāmun **maʿlūm*** (164) — "each of us has a known station". **The same attribute, one of provision and one of station.** This is a textual datum.

### فَوَٰكِهُ وَهُم مُّكْرَمُونَ — fruits and being honoured

***Fawākih* — the plural of *fākiha*, root ف-ك-ه.** The root was analysed in `056-vakia.md` (*fākiha* 56/20, 32; *tafakkahūn* 56/65). **The note there: in the root there is "taking pleasure, delighting the heart"; *fākiha* is not necessary food but something eaten for delight.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded — as my own reading:** the forty-first verse says *rizqun maʿlūm*; the forty-second explains it with a single word: *fawākih.* **What is called "a known provision", then, has as its content not the necessary but the additional.** The word provision suggests subsistence; its explanation turns out to be hospitality.

***Mukramūn* — root ك-ر-م, the passive participle of Form IV: honoured, treated with hospitality.** The original sense of the root is given by the lexicographers as **"generosity and worth"**; *karīm* means both generous and worthy.

**And the word carries the idea of a *host*.** The definition recorded for the word *nuzul* in `056-vakia.md` is at work here too: the reception given to a guest. **In the sixty-second verse the sūra will set these two words (*nuzul* and *zaqqūm*) directly against one another.**

### عَلَىٰ سُرُرٍ مُّتَقَٰبِلِينَ — couches facing one another

***Surur* — the plural of *sarīr*:** a raised seat, a couch, a throne. **The lexicographers record the link between the root س-ر-ر and *surūr* (joy):** a *sarīr* is the seat one sits on in a place of joy. **I give this link as a transmission from the lexicographers.**

***Mutaqābilīn* — root ق-ب-ل, Form VI: to be facing, to stand face to face.** The same root as the *aqbala* of the twenty-seventh verse.

**And the symmetry within the sūra is completed here — this is a textual datum:**

| Verse | Root ق-ب-ل | Who | What they turned for |
|---|---|---|---|
| **27** | *wa **aqbala** baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍin yatasāʾalūn* | Those in the punishment | **To accuse one another** |
| **44** | *ʿalā sururin **mutaqābilīn*** | The mukhlaṣīn | **To sit** |
| **50** | *fa-**aqbala** baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍin yatasāʾalūn* | The mukhlaṣīn | **To talk** |

**The same root three times, and in all three a "turning of the face"; but the work of the turning is different each time.**

**The word *mutaqābilīn* was treated in the same context at `052-tur.md` 52/20 and at `044-duhan.md` 44/53.** The note there holds here too: **to sit facing one another is the lifting of distance and of turned backs.**

---

## 37/45-49 — يُطَافُ عَلَيْهِم بِكَأْسٍ مِّن مَّعِينٍۭ · بَيْضَآءَ لَذَّةٍ لِّلشَّٰرِبِينَ · لَا فِيهَا غَوْلٌ وَلَا هُمْ عَنْهَا يُنزَفُونَ · وَعِندَهُمْ قَٰصِرَٰتُ ٱلطَّرْفِ عِينٌ · كَأَنَّهُنَّ بَيْضٌ مَّكْنُونٌ

*Yuṭāfu ʿalayhim bi-kaʾsin min maʿīn · Bayḍāʾa ladhdhatin li'sh-shāribīn · Lā fīhā ghawlun wa lā hum ʿanhā yunzafūn · Wa ʿindahum qāṣirātu'ṭ-ṭarfi ʿīn · Ka-annahunna bayḍun maknūn*

"A cup filled from a flowing spring is passed round among them; white, a delight for those who drink. In it there is no befuddling, nor are they drained by it. And with them are companions of modest gaze, wide-eyed — as though they were sheltered eggs."

### يُطَافُ عَلَيْهِم — is passed round

**Root ط-و-ف: to go round something.** *Ṭawāf* — going round; *ṭāʾifa* — a circulating group.

**The verb is passive:** *yuṭāfu* — it is passed round. **Who passes it round is not said.** At `052-tur.md` 52/24 this work was plainly given to a group (*wa yaṭūfu ʿalayhim ghilmānun lahum*); **here the agent is concealed.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the passive takes the service out of the scene — attention rests not on the one who serves but on the state of the one served.

***Kaʾs* — a cup, a full vessel.** The lexicographers record a distinction: in Arabic *kaʾs* is used only of a **full** cup; an empty one is called *qadaḥ*. **I give this distinction as a transmission from the lexicographers.**

***Maʿīn* — root م-ع-ن or ع-ي-ن.** Two derivations are transmitted: **(a) from the root م-ع-ن, "flowing water"; (b) from the root ع-ي-ن, "water visible to the eye, issuing from its source".** It occurs elsewhere in the Qurʾān too: *bi-māʾin **maʿīn*** (al-Mulk 67/30), treated in `067-mulk.md`.

**What the two have in common is this, and it is worth noting:** the water **is not standing but flowing.** The forty-fifth verse gives the source, the forty-sixth the colour, the forty-seventh the effect.

### بَيْضَآءَ لَذَّةٍ — white, and a delight

***Bayḍāʾ* — root ب-ي-ض: whiteness.** And the attribute may belong, according to most of the lexicographers, to *kaʾs* or to *maʿīn*. **The disagreement over the case does not markedly change the sense.**

***Ladhdha* — root ل-ذ-ذ: to taste, to please.** **And a point of word order is worth noting: the word is not an adjective but a verbal noun.**

The verse, that is, does not say "delicious" (*ladhīdha*), it says **"a delight"**. In Arabic, when the verbal noun itself is given to a thing in place of an adjective, it is announced that the thing does not merely bear the quality but **is the quality itself** — a way of intensification.

**In English: not "a delicious drink" but "a delight for those who drink".**

**And the note *li'sh-shāribīn* is important:** the delight is defined not in the drink itself but **for the one who drinks.** At `056-vakia.md` 56/55 the drinking of the left-hand side was recounted as *shurba'l-hīm* (the drinking of a camel that is never satisfied). **The two pictures take the same act and tie it to two different outcomes.**

### لَا فِيهَا غَوْلٌ وَلَا هُمْ عَنْهَا يُنزَفُونَ — two negations

**And these two negations remove, separately, the two harms of the drink of this world.**

***Ghawl* — root غ-و-ل.** The original sense the lexicographers give: **to destroy unnoticed, to spoil stealthily.** *Ghāla* — he destroyed unawares; *ightiyāl* — assassination. **From the same root *ghūl* is the name of a creature believed in Arab folklore to lead travellers astray and destroy them** — I mention this name only to show the field of use of the word, not as a transmission of a belief.

**What is transmitted about its sense here:**

| Explanation | What is removed |
|---|---|
| **Headache / befuddlement** | Harm to the body |
| **The loss of reason** | Harm to the mind |
| **Stomach pain / disorder** | Harm to the inner organ |

**All three are transmitted; the root's sense of "stealthy spoiling" covers all three.** I issue no separate ruling.

***Yunzafūn* — root ن-ز-ف.** And the root's original picture: **the water of a well being drawn off until it is exhausted.** *Nazafa'l-biʾr* — the well ran dry. *Nazf* — loss of blood.

**And there are two variant readings, and they change the sense:**

| Reading | Form | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| **يُنزَفُونَ** (*yunzafūn*) | Form IV passive | **They are not drained / their reason is not taken away** |
| **يَنزِفُونَ** (*yanzifūn*) | Form I active | **They do not exhaust it / they do not become drunk** |

**Both are transmitted. I give no reciter's name.** In both readings the two branches of sense (exhaustion / loss of reason) have also been discussed. **I issue no firm ruling.**

**But the structure of the two negations is worth noting — as my own reading:** the first (*ghawl*) removes **the harm coming from the drink**, the second (*yunzafūn*) **what goes out of the drinker.** One from outside in, one from inside out. **Both directions, that is, have been closed.**

### قَٰصِرَٰتُ ٱلطَّرْفِ عِينٌ — of modest gaze, wide-eyed

**The construction *qāṣirātu'ṭ-ṭarf* was treated at `056-vakia.md` 56/35-37, and a connection was made there with Ṣād 38/52 as well (*wa ʿindahum qāṣirātu'ṭ-ṭarfi atrāb*). I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it.**

The summary there: **قَصَرَ — root ق-ص-ر: to shorten a thing, to limit it, to confine it to a place.** *Qaṣr* — a palace (a place one closes oneself into); *maqṣūra* — a partitioned chamber. ***Qāṣirātu'ṭ-ṭarf* — "those who keep their gaze short"**, that is, who do not turn their eyes to another.

***Ṭarf* — root ط-ر-ف: the edge of the eyelid, the tip of the gaze.**

***ʿĪn* — the plural of *ʿaynāʾ*, root ع-ي-ن: having large and beautiful eyes.** The lexicographers record that the concrete origin of the word is **the large-eyed wild ox or the gazelle.** **The construction *ḥūrun ʿīn* was treated at `044-duhan.md` 44/54 and `052-tur.md` 52/20.**

### كَأَنَّهُنَّ بَيْضٌ مَّكْنُونٌ — a sheltered egg

**And this comparison was already cited at `052-tur.md` 52/24** (in the discussion of *luʾluʾun maknūn*), **and this verse was referred to from there. I complete the mutual link here.**

***Maknūn* — root ك-ن-ن: to cover, to keep hidden, to put inside a vessel.** The root was analysed at `056-vakia.md` 56/23 and 56/78. **I do not repeat it.**

***Bayḍ* — an egg.** And there is disagreement over what the comparison is made in respect of:

| View | The respect of the comparison |
|---|---|
| **Colour** | The whiteness of the shell; the faintly yellow-tinged white of the ostrich egg is a measure of beauty in Arabic poetry |
| **Being preserved** | The attribute *maknūn* comes forward: untouched by hand, undusted |
| **Softness / smoothness** | The evenness of the shell |

**All three are transmitted, and the attribute *maknūn* brings the second forward.** I do not impose a preference.

**And a comparison must be recorded — this is a textual datum:**

| Sūra | Comparison | What it is likened to |
|---|---|---|
| **Al-Wāqiʿa 56/23** | *ka-amthāli'l-luʾluʾi'l-maknūn* | **A pearl** |
| **Al-Ṭūr 52/24** | *ka-annahum luʾluʾun maknūn* | **A pearl** (of those who serve) |
| **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/49** | *ka-annahunna **bayḍun** maknūn* | **An egg** |

**The same attribute (*maknūn*), three different things likened.** What is shared is the attribute: **being kept hidden.**

**I record this as my own reading:** a pearl is a comparison of **worth**, an egg a comparison of **untouchedness**. Both are qualified by the same attribute, but the one looks to value and the other to contact.

---

## 37/50-57 — فَأَقْبَلَ بَعْضُهُمْ عَلَىٰ بَعْضٍ يَتَسَآءَلُونَ · قَالَ قَآئِلٌ مِّنْهُمْ إِنِّى كَانَ لِى قَرِينٌ · يَقُولُ أَءِنَّكَ لَمِنَ ٱلْمُصَدِّقِينَ · أَءِذَا مِتْنَا وَكُنَّا تُرَابًا وَعِظَٰمًا أَءِنَّا لَمَدِينُونَ · قَالَ هَلْ أَنتُم مُّطَّلِعُونَ · فَٱطَّلَعَ فَرَءَاهُ فِى سَوَآءِ ٱلْجَحِيمِ · قَالَ تَٱللَّهِ إِن كِدتَّ لَتُرْدِينِ · وَلَوْلَا نِعْمَةُ رَبِّى لَكُنتُ مِنَ ٱلْمُحْضَرِينَ

*Fa-aqbala baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍin yatasāʾalūn · Qāla qāʾilun minhum innī kāna lī qarīn · Yaqūlu a-innaka la-mina'l-muṣaddiqīn · A-idhā mitnā wa kunnā turāban wa ʿiẓāman a-innā la-madīnūn · Qāla hal antum muṭṭaliʿūn · Fa'ṭṭalaʿa fa-raʾāhu fī sawāʾi'l-jaḥīm · Qāla ta'llāhi in kidta la-turdīn · Wa lawlā niʿmatu rabbī la-kuntu mina'l-muḥḍarīn*

"They turn to one another, questioning. One of them says: 'I had a companion who used to say to me: Are you really one of those who hold it true? When we are dead and have become dust and bones, are we really to be called to account?' — He says: 'Will you look?' He looks and sees him in the very middle of the blaze. He says: 'By God, you almost ruined me. Had it not been for my Lord's favour, I too would have been among those brought.'"

### The twin of the scene, and its opposite

**The fiftieth verse is an exact repetition of the twenty-seventh — only the conjunction has changed.** It was given above in a table. **And the content of the two scenes is exactly opposite:**

| | **27-32 (in the fire)** | **50-57 (in the garden)** |
|---|---|---|
| The speaker | Two indefinite groups | **A single person** — *qāʾilun minhum* |
| The subject | Mutual accusation | **A memory** — someone he knew in the past |
| The addressee | The group opposite | Those beside him |
| The closing | *fa-aghwaynākum* — mutual guilt | *lawlā niʿmatu rabbī* — **the source of his own deliverance** |

**I record this as my own reading:** the speech in the fire is turned to **the second person** (*innakum*, *kuntum*) — that is, accusation. The speech in the garden is built in **the first person** (*innī*, *lī*, *la-kuntu*) — that is, narration. **The same sentence opens onto two different modes of speech.**

### قَالَ قَآئِلٌ مِّنْهُمْ — one of them

**A point of word order:** *qāla qāʾilun* — the verb and its subject are from the same root (not م-ف-ع-و-ل but the same root: ق-و-ل). **In Arabic this is called reinforcement by derivation (*ishtiqāq*).**

**And the subject is indefinite: *qāʾilun minhum* — "a speaker from among them".** No name, and one in number. **The scene, that is, descends from a crowd to a single person.**

**As my own reading:** verses forty to forty-nine were a **picture of a group** (they, around them, with them). From the fiftieth verse it passes to **a single voice.** The picture turns into a narrative.

### قَرِين — companion / the one beside

**Root ق-ر-ن: to bind two things together, to pair.** *Qarn* — a horn (what is joined to the head); *qirān* — a joining; *qarīn* — one who is constantly together with another, a close companion.

**The word was treated in `050-kaf.md`** (50/23, 27 — *wa qāla qarīnuhu*), and the use of this word in the scene of the fire was analysed there. **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference here must be recorded:**

| | **Qāf 50/23, 27** | **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/51** |
|---|---|---|
| Who the *qarīn* is | The one who accompanies a person and speaks on the day of reckoning | **A human being he knew in this world** (the widespread explanation) |
| Where he speaks | **At the place of reckoning** | **Recounted in the garden** — he himself is not there |
| What he says | *hādhā mā ladayya ʿatīd* | The words he said in this world are transmitted |

**Whether the *qarīn* here is a human being or another kind of being has been disputed in the classical commentaries. The text does not specify and I issue no ruling.** The content of the speech (denial of resurrection, mockery) accords with both possibilities.

### أَءِنَّكَ لَمِنَ ٱلْمُصَدِّقِينَ — the form of the question

***Muṣaddiq* — root ص-د-ق, the active participle of Form II: one who holds true, who confirms.**

**And the same root had occurred in the thirty-seventh verse:** *wa **ṣaddaqa**'l-mursalīn* — "he confirmed the messengers". **The same verb, two different sides: one who confirms the messengers, and one who mocks those who confirm.** This is a textual datum.

**A point of word order:** the question opens with *a-innaka* — that is, **a reinforced question**. In Arabic this is not a real question for information but announces **incredulity and mockery**. In English: "Come now, do you really believe?"

**And *la-mina'l-muṣaddiqīn*** — "one **of** those who hold it true". The person, that is, is placed in a group. The mockery is not individual but directed at a **category**.

### أَءِذَا مِتْنَا … لَمَدِينُونَ — and its twin within the sūra

**This sentence is a repetition of the sixteenth verse, with one word of difference.** It was given above in a table (in the section on 13-17) and I do not repeat it.

**What is to be added here is this:** in the sixteenth verse this speech was said by **a crowd**; in the fifty-third by **a single person.** And in the fifty-fifth verse that person **is shown.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the sūra gives a sentence twice, and at the second giving adds a **face** to it. The objection in the sixteenth verse belonged to an abstract crowd; the objection in the fifty-third belongs to a particular person standing in the middle of the blaze.

### هَلْ أَنتُم مُّطَّلِعُونَ — will you look?

**Root ط-ل-ع: a thing's coming out, rising and becoming visible.** *Ṭulūʿ* — rising; *maṭlaʿ* — the place of rising; *ṭalīʿa* — a vanguard. **The root was treated at `050-kaf.md` 50/10 (*wa'n-nakhla bāsiqātin lahā ṭalʿun naḍīd*) and in `104-humeze.md` (*taṭṭaliʿu ʿala'l-afʾida*).**

***Muṭṭaliʿ* — the active participle of Form VIII: one who looks from above, who glances into a place.** The pattern announces **looking from a height downward.**

**And at `040-gafir.md` 40/36-37 the same root occurred in Pharaoh's mouth:** *fa-**aṭṭaliʿa** ilā ilāhi Mūsā* — "that I may look upon the god of Moses". **The same verb, two directions: one wants to climb up and look, the other looks down from above.** This is a textual observation.

**A point of word order:** the question is *hal antum muṭṭaliʿūn* — "are you people who look?" **It is asked with a nominal sentence.** Not "will you look?", then, but "are you in a state to look?"

**And note: the question is put to those beside him, but in the next verse the one who looks is a single person:** *fa'ṭṭalaʿa* (singular). **The offer is in the plural, the act in the singular.** This is an observation about the structure.

### فَرَءَاهُ فِى سَوَآءِ ٱلْجَحِيمِ — in the very middle

***Sawāʾ* — root س-و-ي: equality, equivalence.** *Sawāʾu'sh-shayʾ* — the very middle of a thing; because the middle point is **equidistant** from the edges. **The word's sense of "middle" arises from this equality.**

**The same construction occurs in the Qurʾān at al-Dukhān 44/47:** *fa'tilūhu ilā **sawāʾi**'l-jaḥīm.* **Treated in `044-duhan.md`.**

**As my own reading:** it is not said "in the blaze" but **"in the very middle of the blaze"**. Not the edge but the centre. And what was offered in the fifty-fourth verse was **looking** — the speaker, that is, sees his companion's place with his own eye.

### تَٱللَّهِ إِن كِدتَّ لَتُرْدِينِ — you almost

***Ta'llāhi* — an oath with the letter *tāʾ*.** In Arabic *wāw*, *bāʾ* and *tāʾ* are particles of oath; **and *tāʾ* is used only with the word "God".** The lexicographers record that the *tāʾ* oath also carries the sense of **astonishment and of an unlooked-for state.**

***In kidta* — here *in* is not the negative but the *mukhaffafa mina'th-thaqīla*** (the lightened form of the heavy *inna*). The grammarians know it by the presence of the *lām*: *la-turdīn*. **The sense: "you really did almost…"**

***Kāda* — the verb in Arabic announcing "to be near": to be on the point of.**

***Turdīn* — root ر-د-ي: to fall from a height, to perish.** *Taraddā* — he fell, he perished. In the Qurʾān: *wa mā yughnī ʿanhu māluhu idhā **taraddā*** (al-Layl 92/11) — **treated in `092-leyl.md`.**

**And the picture of the word is worth noting:** ruin is described as **falling** — that is, from a height downward. **And the speaker is at that very moment looking down from above** (*muṭṭaliʿ*). The scene and the word overlap. **I record this as an observation.**

### وَلَوْلَا نِعْمَةُ رَبِّى — and the source of the deliverance

**And the block closes with an admission: *"Had it not been for my Lord's favour…"***

***Lawlā*** — in Arabic the conditional particle announcing "had it not been for"; it announces a possibility that was not realised.

***Muḥḍarīn* — root ح-ض-ر, the passive participle of Form IV: those brought, those made to be present.** **And this word occurs three times in the sūra:**

| Verse | Who | State |
|---|---|---|
| **57** | The speaker | *la-kuntu mina'l-muḥḍarīn* — **I would have been (but was not)** |
| **127** | Those who denied Elijah | *fa-innahum **la-muḥḍarūn*** — **they will be** |
| **158** | Those who ascribe kinship to the jinn | *innahum **la-muḥḍarūn*** — **they will be** |

**All three occurrences are in the same mould. Only the first carries a difference: there the verb stands inside an unrealised condition.** This is a fact that can be verified from the text.

**The choice of the word is worth noting:** *iḥḍār* is to **bring** someone into the presence of an authority. The word announces, that is, not the punishment but **the summons.** In all three places the sūra names not the penalty itself but the procedure that leads to it.

---

## 37/58-61 — أَفَمَا نَحْنُ بِمَيِّتِينَ · إِلَّا مَوْتَتَنَا ٱلْأُولَىٰ وَمَا نَحْنُ بِمُعَذَّبِينَ · إِنَّ هَٰذَا لَهُوَ ٱلْفَوْزُ ٱلْعَظِيمُ · لِمِثْلِ هَٰذَا فَلْيَعْمَلِ ٱلْعَٰمِلُونَ

*A-fa-mā naḥnu bi-mayyitīn · Illā mawtatana'l-ūlā wa mā naḥnu bi-muʿadhdhabīn · Inna hādhā la-huwa'l-fawzu'l-ʿaẓīm · Li-mithli hādhā fa'l-yaʿmali'l-ʿāmilūn*

"'So then we are not to die — save our first death? And we are not to be punished?' — This indeed is the great triumph. For the like of this let the workers work."

### أَفَمَا نَحْنُ بِمَيِّتِينَ — whose the question is

**And here there is a disagreement: who says these two verses?**

| View | Who says it | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| **The continuation of the speaker** | The speech begun in the fifty-first verse continues; after looking at his companion the man turns and asks those beside him | The pronoun is plural (*naḥnu*) and the preceding speech also belongs to this person |
| **The common word of those in the garden** | They all say it together | *Naḥnu* is plural |
| **Said to the one in the fire** | The speaker turns to the one below and says "we are not to die" | The continuation of the scene |

**The text does not specify; all three are transmitted. I do not impose a preference.**

**A point of word order:** the question opens with *a-fa-mā* — interrogative particle + *fāʾ* + negation. **These three set up a *question of astonishment* attached to what precedes.** In English: "So then…?"

**And the question is not a real question — because its answer is known.** In Arabic this is called **the interrogative of astonishment** (*istifhām al-taʿajjub*): to say again, with joy or with wonder, something already known.

**I record this as my own reading:** the man has just seen someone in the middle of the blaze. **The source of the question is what he has seen.** He perceives his own state anew by comparing it with the state he has seen.

### إِلَّا مَوْتَتَنَا ٱلْأُولَىٰ — the first death

***Mawta* — root م-و-ت, the *marra* pattern announcing "a single dying"** (the *faʿla* pattern announces a single occurrence). Not *mawt* (death), that is, but ***mawta*** (one event of dying).

***Al-ūlā* — "the first".** **And the construct sets up a logic:** saying "the first" appears to require the existence of a "second" — but the verse negates the second.

**The explanation transmitted in the classical commentaries:** *al-ūlā* here is not an ordinal number but means **"that death which lies in the past"** — that is, death in this world. There is a similar expression at al-Dukhān 44/56: *lā yadhūqūna fīha'l-mawta **illa'l-mawtata'l-ūlā***, **treated in `044-duhan.md`. I rely on that treatment.**

### وَمَا نَحْنُ بِمُعَذَّبِينَ — and the second negation

**Two negations side by side: no death, no punishment.**

**And the order of the two is worth noting — as my own reading:** first the **duration** is secured (we shall not die), then the **state** (we shall not be punished). The two conditions of a favour: that it last and that it not be spoilt. **The same pair had been made for the drink in the forty-seventh verse:** it neither harms (*ghawl*) nor is exhausted (*yunzafūn*). **In both places the same logic: no spoiling from without, no ending from within.**

***Muʿadhdhab* — root ع-ذ-ب, the passive participle of Form II.** The lexicographers record an oddity: from the same root *ʿadhb* means **sweet water**. **Different explanations are transmitted about the link between the two senses** — on one view *ʿadhāb* is what **cuts off** a sweet life. **I give this link as a transmission; I issue no firm etymological ruling.**

### إِنَّ هَٰذَا لَهُوَ ٱلْفَوْزُ ٱلْعَظِيمُ — the ruling

**And here the speaking party changes:** verses fifty-eight and fifty-nine were a question; the sixtieth is a **ruling**. **In the classical commentaries it is widely said that this sentence is the text's own voice.**

**The word order is very strong: three reinforcements one upon another.**

| Element | Its work |
|---|---|
| **إِنَّ** | The particle of reinforcement |
| **لَـ** | *Lām al-ibtidāʾ* — a second reinforcement |
| **هُوَ** | *Ḍamīr al-faṣl* (the separating pronoun) — "this is it, and not another" |

***Ḍamīr al-faṣl*** in Arabic sets up **restriction** (*ḥaṣr*) by separating the subject from the predicate. **The sentence says, that is, not "this is a great triumph" but "the great triumph is precisely this, and nothing else".**

***Fawz* — root ف-و-ز.** The original sense the lexicographers give: **to escape and to attain what one desires** — the two together. *Fāza* — he both escaped danger and obtained what he wanted. *Mafāza* — both a place of deliverance and a perilous crossing in the desert.

**And that both senses are at work here is worth noting:** the fifty-ninth verse recounted **escaping** (no death, no punishment), verses forty-one to forty-nine **attaining** (provision, hospitality, companions). **The word gathers the two under one heading.**

### لِمِثْلِ هَٰذَا فَلْيَعْمَلِ ٱلْعَٰمِلُونَ — and a single command

**And the block closes with the only command in this part of the sūra.**

**A point of word order — and an important one:** *li-mithli hādhā* has been brought forward. The ordinary order would have been *fa'l-yaʿmali'l-ʿāmilūna li-mithli hādhā*. **In Arabic bringing forward (*taqdīm*) sets up *ḥaṣr*: "let them work for this, and not for anything else."**

***Li-mithli hādhā* — "for the like of this".** **And note: not *li-hādhā* (for this) but *li-mithli hādhā* (for the like of this).**

**Two explanations are transmitted in the classical commentaries for this expression's being built with the word *mithl*:**

| Explanation | What it says |
|---|---|
| **Intensification** | In Arabic *mithl* is sometimes added for reinforcement and means "exactly this" (as in the instance *laysa ka-mithlihi shayʾ*) |
| **Real likeness** | The picture recounted is an example; what is to be worked for is not this picture itself but an outcome of the kind it indicates |

**Both are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

***Fa'l-yaʿmal* — a command to the third person with the imperative *lām*.** Not "work", then, but **"let the workers work".**

**And the verb and its subject are from the same root:** *yaʿmali'l-**ʿāmilūn***. The same structure as *qāla qāʾilun* in the fifty-first verse. **In this section the sūra uses a verb and subject from the same root twice.** This is a textual datum.

**I record this as my own reading:** the sentence is not a summons but a **correction of direction**. "The workers" already exist and are already working; the verse does not command working, it changes **the aim of the working.** In the thirty-ninth verse it had been said *"you are requited only for what you did"* — here the same verb (ع-م-ل) is named together with its aim.

---

## 37/62-68 — أَذَٰلِكَ خَيْرٌ نُّزُلًا أَمْ شَجَرَةُ ٱلزَّقُّومِ · إِنَّا جَعَلْنَٰهَا فِتْنَةً لِّلظَّٰلِمِينَ · إِنَّهَا شَجَرَةٌ تَخْرُجُ فِىٓ أَصْلِ ٱلْجَحِيمِ · طَلْعُهَا كَأَنَّهُۥ رُءُوسُ ٱلشَّيَٰطِينِ · فَإِنَّهُمْ لَءَاكِلُونَ مِنْهَا فَمَالِـُٔونَ مِنْهَا ٱلْبُطُونَ · ثُمَّ إِنَّ لَهُمْ عَلَيْهَا لَشَوْبًا مِّنْ حَمِيمٍ · ثُمَّ إِنَّ مَرْجِعَهُمْ لَإِلَى ٱلْجَحِيمِ

*A-dhālika khayrun nuzulan am shajaratu'z-zaqqūm · Innā jaʿalnāhā fitnatan li'ẓ-ẓālimīn · Innahā shajaratun takhruju fī aṣli'l-jaḥīm · Ṭalʿuhā ka-annahu ruʾūsu'sh-shayāṭīn · Fa-innahum la-ākilūna minhā fa-māliʾūna mina'l-buṭūn · Thumma inna lahum ʿalayhā la-shawban min ḥamīm · Thumma inna marjiʿahum la-ila'l-jaḥīm*

"Is this the better hospitality, or the tree of *zaqqūm*? We have made it a trial for the wrongdoers. It is a tree that comes forth in the very bottom of the blaze; its spathes are as though they were the heads of satans. They will eat of it and fill their bellies with it. Then upon it they will have a draught mixed with scalding water. Then their return is again to the blaze."

### أَذَٰلِكَ خَيْرٌ نُّزُلًا — the word of the comparison

**The word *nuzul* was analysed in detail at `056-vakia.md` 56/56 (*hādhā nuzuluhum yawma'd-dīn*) and 56/93 (*fa-nuzulun min ḥamīm*). I do not repeat it.**

**The definition there — and the whole weight of the comparison here rests on it:** *nuzul*, according to the lexicographers, is **the first hospitality set out for a guest on his arrival.** Not the feast itself, but **what is offered while receiving him at the door.**

**And what was recorded in the section on al-Wāqiʿa turns here into a question.** There the word had been used **for one side only** (the hospitality of the left-hand side, twice). **In al-Ṣāffāt the word becomes a *measure of comparison*:**

| Sūra | Use | Its function |
|---|---|---|
| **Al-Wāqiʿa 56/56** | *hādhā nuzuluhum* — a statement | **A seal** — it closes the block |
| **Al-Wāqiʿa 56/93** | *fa-nuzulun min ḥamīm* — a statement | **A seal** — it approaches the closing of the sūra |
| **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/62** | *a-dhālika khayrun **nuzulan*** — **a question** | **A measure** — it compares two pictures |

**The same word, then, does two different pieces of work in two sūras: in the one it seals the ruling, in the other it makes the ruling be asked for.** This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

**A point of word order:** *nuzulan* is in the accusative and the grammarians count it as a **specifier** (*tamyīz*): "which is better — *in respect of being hospitality*?" **The specifier determines in what respect the comparison is made.** The question is not "which is better?" but **"which is better as hospitality?"**

**And this ties to the forty-second verse:** *wa hum **mukramūn*** — "and they will be honoured". **The root ك-ر-م and the word *nuzul* are in the same field: hospitality.** The sūra sets up the idea of hospitality in the forty-second verse and makes it a measure in the sixty-second.

**And the form of the question is worth noting:** *a-dhālika khayrun … am …* — **a two-sided question** (*istifhām al-taʿalluq*). This is the same mould as *a-hum ashaddu khalqan **am** man khalaqnā* in the eleventh verse. **Twice the sūra asks in the same form and twice it leaves the answer to the addressee.**

### شَجَرَةُ ٱلزَّقُّومِ — a difference of definiteness

**The word *zaqqūm* and the disagreement over its root were treated in detail at `056-vakia.md` 56/52 — including the table of the disagreement over the root. I do not repeat it.** The three views there (from the Arabic root *tazaqqama*; the name of a known plant; of foreign origin) were given there, and no firm etymological ruling was issued there either. **That note holds here too.**

**The only thing to be added here is the difference of definiteness, and this is a textual datum:**

| Sūra | Expression | Definiteness |
|---|---|---|
| **Al-Wāqiʿa 56/52** | *min shajarin min zaqqūm* | **Indefinite** — "from a tree, from *zaqqūm*" |
| **Al-Dukhān 44/43** | *shajarata'z-zaqqūm* | **Definite** — in a construct |
| **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/62** | *shajaratu'z-zaqqūm* | **Definite** — in a construct |

**This comparison was already made at `044-duhan.md` 44/43-44, and the difference between al-Wāqiʿa and al-Dukhān was given there in a table. I rely on that treatment.**

**That al-Ṣāffāt uses the same form as al-Dukhān is worth recording.** In al-Wāqiʿa the word **did not identify**; here and in al-Dukhān it **does identify** — because in both, information is given about the tree (where it grows, what it resembles, what is done with it).

### إِنَّا جَعَلْنَٰهَا فِتْنَةً لِّلظَّٰلِمِينَ — a trial

***Fitna* — root ف-ت-ن.** And the concrete picture of the root is worth noting: **to put gold into the fire and separate the pure from the dross.** *Fatana'dh-dhahab* — he tested gold with fire. **The root was analysed at `051-zariyat.md` 51/13-14 (*yawma hum ʿala'n-nāri yuftanūn*). I rely on that treatment.**

**And there is disagreement over the use here:**

| Explanation | What *fitna* means | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| **A matter of dispute / an occasion of objection** | When the news of the tree was given the deniers objected: "does a tree grow inside fire?" — and so the report became a point of division | The root's sense of "separating"; and that the expression *al-shajarata'l-malʿūnata fi'l-Qurʾān* at al-Isrāʾ 17/60 is named as a *fitna* |
| **Punishment / testing** | A punishment and a trial for the wrongdoers | The root's sense of "putting into fire" |
| **Both at once** | A trial as a report in this world, a testing as a punishment in the hereafter | The two senses working together |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** Both branches of the root (separating / testing by fire) are at work in this context.

### إِنَّهَا شَجَرَةٌ تَخْرُجُ فِىٓ أَصْلِ ٱلْجَحِيمِ — a tree growing at the bottom

***Aṣl* — root أ-ص-ل: the root of a thing, its bottom, what it rests on.** *Aṣīl* — the hour after the afternoon (the bottom of the day); *aṣl* — the root of a tree.

**And the construct is odd — the lexicographers record this:** *takhruju **fī** aṣli'l-jaḥīm* — "it comes forth **in** the bottom of the blaze". **The expected preposition would have been *min*** (it comes forth from its bottom). **The use of *fī* announces that the coming forth takes place *inside*:** the tree does not come out, it grows within the fire.

**I record this as my own reading:** the choice of words sets up a reversed garden. **A tree normally grows in soil and rises with water; here it grows in fire.** And because the word *aṣl* means both "root" and "bottom", in the sentence the root of the tree and the bottom of the blaze fall in the same place.

### طَلْعُهَا كَأَنَّهُۥ رُءُوسُ ٱلشَّيَٰطِينِ — and the linguistic dispute

***Ṭalʿ* — root ط-ل-ع: what comes out, what appears.** In the palm tree, **the spathe of the bud**, the first state in which the fruit appears. **The root was treated at `050-kaf.md` 50/10 (*lahā ṭalʿun naḍīd*), where it was used of the palm bud.**

**The sūra, that is, uses on the side of the fire the word used in describing the plants on the side of the garden. This is a textual datum.**

### Likening to something never seen — a linguistic dispute

**And here there is a question the classical lexicographers dwelt on at length: *the heads of satans* is a thing no one has seen. How can a comparison be made with something never seen?**

**The way a comparison (*tashbīh*) works is this:** the *mushabbah* (what is likened) may be unknown, but the *mushabbah bihi* (what it is likened to) must be **known** — because the work of a comparison is to build a bridge from the known to the unknown. **Here the thing likened to appears itself to be unknown. That is the objection.**

**The answers given in the classical period. I give them in a table and impose no preference:**

| Answer | What it says | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| **A likening to what is in the mind** | The comparison is made not to an object in the outer world but **to the image of horror settled in the addressee's mind.** In Arabic it was common to say "like a satan" of ugliness; the comparison rests on this settled usage | **The most defended explanation.** Its ground is that the work of a comparison is not to *give information* but to *produce an effect*: it calls up an image already present in the hearer |
| ***Shayṭān* is the name of a plant** | It is transmitted that in Arabia a bitter, ugly-looking plant was called "satans' heads" (*ruʾūsu'sh-shayāṭīn*) | It is transmitted, but there is no certain knowledge about how widespread this name was |
| ***Shayṭān* is a kind of snake** | It is recorded that in Arabic a kind of ugly and fearsome snake was called *shayṭān* | It occurs among the lexicographers; but it risks conflicting with the sense in which the Qurʾān uses the word elsewhere |
| **The unknowability is itself the point** | The work of the comparison is not to describe but to announce **indescribability**. To liken a thing to something unknown is a way of saying "you cannot picture this" | It accords with the "estranging" function recorded in the section on al-Wāqiʿa for the indefiniteness of *zaqqūm* |

**And a note is required — as my own reading:** the first and fourth explanations do not exclude one another. **The comparison both calls up a settled image of fear and refuses to describe.** The English "like a monster" does the same work: neither speaker nor hearer has seen a monster, yet the expression works.

**A point of word order and pronoun agreement:** *ṭalʿuhā **ka-annahu** ruʾūsu'sh-shayāṭīn* — the pronoun is **masculine singular** (*hu*), while the predicate is **plural** (*ruʾūs*). The grammarians resolve this thus: the pronoun refers to the word *ṭalʿ* (masculine singular), and because *ṭalʿ* is a collective noun its predicate can come in the plural. **Not "each of its buds", then, but "its buds taken together".**

### فَإِنَّهُمْ لَءَاكِلُونَ مِنْهَا فَمَالِـُٔونَ مِنْهَا ٱلْبُطُونَ — the repetition of al-Wāqiʿa

**And this sentence is an almost exact repetition of `056-vakia.md` 56/52-53. It was analysed there and I do not repeat it.**

The note there: **the root م-ل-أ is to fill; the verse does not say "they will be satisfied" but "they will fill their bellies". To be satisfied is a satiation, to fill is a matter of volume.**

**The difference between the two sūras in a table:**

| | **Al-Wāqiʿa 56/51-53** | **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/66** |
|---|---|---|
| **Person** | *inna**kum*** — second person | *inna**hum*** — third person |
| **The tree** | *min shajarin min zaqqūm* — indefinite | *shajaratu'z-zaqqūm* — definite (62) |
| **The chain** | Four *fāʾ*: eating → filling → drinking → drinking like an animal | **Two *fāʾ*, then two *thumma***: eating → filling → **then** the mixture → **then** the return |

**The last row is the most important difference, and it is countable.** In al-Wāqiʿa the whole scene ran with *fāʾ* — without interval. **In al-Ṣāffāt, after two *fāʾ*, *thumma* comes twice.**

***Thumma* in Arabic announces *tarākhī* (time coming between); *fāʾ* announces the absence of interval.** **In al-Ṣāffāt, that is, the scene is a scene with pauses:** after eating and filling they drink, and after that they are taken back.

**I record this as my own reading:** the chain of al-Wāqiʿa ran **without drawing breath**; the chain of al-Ṣāffāt is built **with stops.** The two sūras make two different rhythms out of the same material.

### شَوْبًا مِّنْ حَمِيمٍ — a mixture

***Shawb* — root ش-و-ب: to mix a thing with something else.** *Shāba* — he mixed; *shāʾiba* — a foreign element that gets into a thing, a taint. **The Turkish word *şaibe* ("taint") comes from this root.**

**And the word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.**

**In al-Wāqiʿa it was said *ʿalayhi mina'l-ḥamīm* — "upon it, of scalding water".** Here *shawban min ḥamīm* — **"a draught mixed with scalding water".**

| | **Al-Wāqiʿa 56/54** | **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/67** |
|---|---|---|
| The expression | *fa-shāribūna ʿalayhi mina'l-ḥamīm* | *inna lahum ʿalayhā la-**shawban** min ḥamīm* |
| Is there a verb | **Yes** — *shāribūn* (they drink) | **No** — a nominal sentence: "for them there is…" |
| The word | *ḥamīm* directly | ***Shawb*** — a mixture |

**As my own reading:** the addition of the word *shawb* announces that what is drunk **is not pure.** In the forty-sixth verse *bayḍāʾ* (pure white) was said of the other side — that is, **unmixed.** The drink of the two sides is distinguished by the same measure: the one pure, the other mixed.

***Ḥamīm* — root ح-م-م: to become extremely hot.** The root was treated at `056-vakia.md` 56/42, 54, 93, and it was recorded there that all three occurrences belong to the same group.

### ثُمَّ إِنَّ مَرْجِعَهُمْ لَإِلَى ٱلْجَحِيمِ — the return

***Marjiʿ* — root ر-ج-ع: to return.** A noun of place / a *maṣdar mīmī*: **the place of return, the returning.**

**And the sentence closes a circle — this is a textual datum:**

| Verse | Where *al-jaḥīm* occurs |
|---|---|
| **64** | The tree comes forth **from the bottom of the blaze** |
| **68** | Their return is **to the blaze** |

**What they eat comes out of the blaze, and they themselves return to the blaze.** The tree does not come out, and neither do they.

**And an oddity in the word order must be recorded:** *la-**ilā**'l-jaḥīm* — the reinforcing *lām* has come at the head of a preposition. The grammarians count this as rare but permissible. **Its effect is this: the emphasis falls not on the place of return but on the *direction* of the returning.**

**I record this as my own reading:** in the sixty-second verse a question had been asked — "which is the better hospitality?" **The sixty-eighth verse does not give the answer; it shows that the answer is unnecessary.** For seven verses what the second option is has been recounted, and the comparison has finished of itself.

---

## 37/69-74 — إِنَّهُمْ أَلْفَوْا۟ ءَابَآءَهُمْ ضَآلِّينَ · فَهُمْ عَلَىٰٓ ءَاثَٰرِهِمْ يُهْرَعُونَ · وَلَقَدْ ضَلَّ قَبْلَهُمْ أَكْثَرُ ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ · وَلَقَدْ أَرْسَلْنَا فِيهِم مُّنذِرِينَ · فَٱنظُرْ كَيْفَ كَانَ عَٰقِبَةُ ٱلْمُنذَرِينَ · إِلَّا عِبَادَ ٱللَّهِ ٱلْمُخْلَصِينَ

*Innahum alfaw ābāʾahum ḍāllīn · Fa-hum ʿalā āthārihim yuhraʿūn · Wa laqad ḍalla qablahum aktharu'l-awwalīn · Wa laqad arsalnā fīhim mundhirīn · Fa'nẓur kayfa kāna ʿāqibatu'l-mundharīn · Illā ʿibāda'llāhi'l-mukhlaṣīn*

"They found their forefathers astray, and they are rushed along in their tracks. Before them most of those of old had gone astray. And We had sent warners among them. Look, then, how the end of those warned turned out — except God's chosen servants."

### أَلْفَوْا۟ — they found

**Root ل-ف-و (also recorded as أ-ل-ف-و): to come upon a thing, to meet with it, to find.** Form IV: *alfā* — he found, he came upon.

**And the choice of the verb is worth noting.** *Wajada* (he found) could also have been said. **The difference the lexicographers record:** *alfā* announces **meeting by chance, without searching.** In the Qurʾān: *wa **alfayā** sayyidahā lada'l-bāb* (Yūsuf 12/25) — "and they came upon her husband at the door".

**I record this as my own reading:** the verb announces that the finding **was not their own choice.** It is a state they found ready where they were born. **And it is not this that the verse blames; what is blamed is in the next verse: continuing without question what they found.**

***Ḍāllīn* — root ض-ل-ل: to lose the way.** The root was analysed in `001-fatiha.md` (*wa la'ḍ-ḍāllīn*). **The note there: the concrete picture of the root is losing the way in the desert; one who is lost need not be of ill intent.**

**A point of word order:** *ḍāllīn* stands as a circumstantial — "they found them **in a state of having gone astray**". **What is qualified, that is, is not the persons they found but the *state* they found.**

### يُهْرَعُونَ — they are rushed along

**Root ه-ر-ع.** And the word is worth noting: **the verb is in the passive pattern but its sense is active.**

**The sense the lexicographers give: *ihrāʿ* — to walk fast, in haste, as though being pushed.** *Yuhraʿūn* — they are hurried along, they are dragged.

**It occurs once more in the Qurʾān:** *wa jāʾahu qawmuhu **yuhraʿūna** ilayh* (Hūd 11/78) — the people of Lot came **rushing** toward him.

**And the passive is at work here — I record this as my own reading:** the verse does not say "they run", it says **"they are rushed along"**. Who does the rushing is not said. **The movement, that is, is not their own.** In the sixty-ninth verse there was a state they found; in the seventieth that state carries them.

***ʿAlā āthārihim* — "upon their tracks".** ***Athar* — root أ-ث-ر: the mark a thing leaves behind, a footprint.** The root was treated at `040-gafir.md` 40/21 (*wa āthāran fi'l-arḍ*).

**And the preposition *ʿalā* (upon) is worth noting:** not *fī* (in) or *ilā* (toward). ***ʿAlā*** announces treading on a surface. **They go, that is, treading in the footprints.**

**This is the most widespread mould of the theme of following the forefathers in the Qurʾān**, and it was treated in detail at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/22-23 (*innā wajadnā ābāʾanā ʿalā ummatin wa innā ʿalā āthārihim muhtadūn*). **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference here:** in al-Zukhruf **they themselves said** this ("we are rightly guided in their tracks"); here **the text says it about them**, and the verb is passive. **In al-Zukhruf a claim, then; here a diagnosis.**

### وَلَقَدْ ضَلَّ قَبْلَهُمْ أَكْثَرُ ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ — number is not a proof

***Akthar* — "most".** And the choice of this word is worth noting.

**The verse does not say "all". *Aktharu'l-awwalīn* — "most of those of old".**

**I record this as my own reading:** the word does two pieces of work. **One:** that the majority went astray shows that the majority is not the measure of truth — it breaks the proof from the forefathers directly. **Two:** saying "most" also announces that a minority did not go astray. **And that minority will be named in the seventy-fourth verse:** *illā ʿibāda'llāhi'l-mukhlaṣīn.*

**The six-verse block thus sets up a logic of number:** the majority went astray, the minority was saved, and the warner went to both.

***Al-awwalūn* — for the third time in the sūra.** 17 (*ābāʾuna'l-awwalūn*), 71 (here), 126 (*rabba ābāʾikumu'l-awwalīn* — from Elijah's mouth), 168 (*dhikran mina'l-awwalīn*). **Four occurrences, four different pieces of work.**

| Verse | Expression | Who says it | Its work |
|---|---|---|---|
| **17** | *a-wa ābāʾuna'l-awwalūn* | The deniers | To enlarge the objection |
| **71** | *aktharu'l-awwalīn* | The text | To announce that the majority went astray |
| **126** | *rabba ābāʾikumu'l-awwalīn* | **Elijah** | To bind the forefathers too to the same Lord |
| **168** | *dhikran mina'l-awwalīn* | The deniers | To ask for something that does not exist |

**The hundred and twenty-sixth verse is the most interesting place in this table, and I will treat it separately there:** Elijah does not reject following the forefathers — **he says that the forefathers too had the same Lord.** **The sūra, that is, takes up the question of following the forefathers from two directions: it rejects blind following, but it does not condemn the forefathers wholesale either.**

### وَلَقَدْ أَرْسَلْنَا فِيهِم مُّنذِرِينَ — warners

***Mundhir* — root ن-ذ-ر, the active participle of Form IV: one who warns, who announces a bad outcome in advance.** The root was treated in `077-murselat.md`, in `054-kamer.md` (the most frequent word of that sūra: 11 times) and in `074-muddessir.md`. **I do not repeat it.**

**The basic note there: *indhār* is not to frighten but *to give notice in advance*** — to announce while there is still time.

**A point of word order:** *arsalnā **fīhim*** — "We sent **among** them". Not *ilayhim* (to them). ***Fī*** announces an inclusion: the warner is not a stranger coming from outside but **from among them.** **The same emphasis was present at Qāf 50/2:** *mundhirun **minhum*** ("a warner from among themselves"), treated in `050-kaf.md`.

***Mundhirīn* is an indefinite plural: "warners".** How many they were and who they were is not said. **And this indefiniteness will be removed from the seventy-fifth verse onward:** the sequence will begin and the names will be given one by one.

**I record this as my own reading:** the seventy-second verse is the **heading** of the sūra's sequence of prophets. It says "We sent warners"; three verses later the names of those warners begin to be enumerated. **The command *fa'nẓur* (look) in the seventy-third verse supports this too: what is to be looked at comes immediately after.**

### فَٱنظُرْ كَيْفَ كَانَ عَٰقِبَةُ ٱلْمُنذَرِينَ — and the turning of the word

**And the sharpest point in the word order is here:**

| Verse | Word | Pattern | Its sense |
|---|---|---|---|
| **72** | **مُنذِرِين** (*mundhirīn*) | **Active** participle | **Those who warn** |
| **73** | **ٱلْمُنذَرِين** (*mundharīn*) | **Passive** participle | **Those who are warned** |

**The same root, two consecutive verses, a difference of a single vowel: kasra / fatḥa.** And the sense turns about.

**This is a linguistic fact that can be verified from the text and is one of the most elegant places in the sūra.**

**And the word will turn once more at the end of the sūra:** *fa-sāʾa ṣabāḥu'l-**mundharīn*** (177) — "how evil is the morning of those who were warned". **The same passive participle pattern.** This is a countable repetition.

***ʿĀqiba* — root ع-ق-ب: to come after a thing; the heel.** *ʿAqib* — the heel; *ʿāqiba* — the outcome, what comes behind a matter. **The root was treated at `040-gafir.md` 40/21 (*kayfa kāna ʿāqibatu'lladhīna kānū min qablihim*).**

***Fa'nẓur* — root ن-ظ-ر, an imperative: look.** And the addressee is singular. **The command is given, that is, not to a crowd but to one person — to the Prophet, or to each single reader.**

### إِلَّا عِبَادَ ٱللَّهِ ٱلْمُخْلَصِينَ — the second occurrence

**The sentence occurs for the second time in the sūra (40, 74). It was analysed in the section on the fortieth verse; I do not repeat it.**

**The question of word order here must be recorded:** what does the exception attach to? **In the seventy-third verse there was *al-mundharīn* (those warned); in the seventy-first, *aktharu'l-awwalīn* (most of those of old).**

| Attachment | Sense |
|---|---|
| **To 71** (*aktharu'l-awwalīn*) | "Most went astray — except God's chosen servants" |
| **To 73** (*al-mundharīn*) | "Look at the end of those warned — except God's chosen servants" |

**Both are transmitted by the grammarians; the sense arrives at the same place either way.** I do not impose a preference.

**And the closing of the block is worth recording — as my own reading:** the seventy-fourth verse is identical with the fortieth, and thirty-four verses lie between them. **Blocks III and IV are thus framed by the same sentence: one opens, the other closes.** And inside that frame there are two pictures — the garden and *zaqqūm*. **The frame holds the two pictures together.**

---

## 37/75-82 — وَلَقَدْ نَادَىٰنَا نُوحٌ فَلَنِعْمَ ٱلْمُجِيبُونَ · وَنَجَّيْنَٰهُ وَأَهْلَهُۥ مِنَ ٱلْكَرْبِ ٱلْعَظِيمِ · وَجَعَلْنَا ذُرِّيَّتَهُۥ هُمُ ٱلْبَاقِينَ · وَتَرَكْنَا عَلَيْهِ فِى ٱلْءَاخِرِينَ · سَلَٰمٌ عَلَىٰ نُوحٍ فِى ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ · إِنَّا كَذَٰلِكَ نَجْزِى ٱلْمُحْسِنِينَ · إِنَّهُۥ مِنْ عِبَادِنَا ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ · ثُمَّ أَغْرَقْنَا ٱلْءَاخَرِينَ

*Wa laqad nādānā Nūḥun fa-la-niʿma'l-mujībūn · Wa najjaynāhu wa ahlahu mina'l-karbi'l-ʿaẓīm · Wa jaʿalnā dhurriyyatahu humu'l-bāqīn · Wa taraknā ʿalayhi fi'l-ākhirīn · Salāmun ʿalā Nūḥin fi'l-ʿālamīn · Innā kadhālika najzi'l-muḥsinīn · Innahu min ʿibādina'l-muʾminīn · Thumma aghraqna'l-ākharīn*

"And Noah called upon Us — and how excellent are We as answerers. We saved him and his household from the great distress. We made his progeny the ones who remained. And We left upon him, among those who came after: 'Peace be upon Noah among all the worlds.' Thus do We requite those who do good. He was one of Our believing servants. Then We drowned the others."

### The beginning of the sequence

**Eight verses, and within them not a full account — a *summary*.** What Noah said to his people, how long he waited, the building of the ark, the flood — none of it is recounted. **What is recounted is only this: he called, he was saved, his line continued, his name remained.**

**This is the method the sūra keeps to in its sequence of prophets, and it is worth recording:** the accounts are given **not as story but as outcome.** **Comparing this with Sūrat Nūḥ, treated in `071-nuh.md`, makes it clear:** there, for twenty-eight verses, Noah's case and his call are recounted; **here the case itself is wholly absent, and only its result is present.**

### وَلَقَدْ نَادَىٰنَا نُوحٌ — he called

***Nādā* — root ن-د-و/ن-د-ي: to call to one who is far off.** The root was treated at `068-kalem.md` 68/48 (*idh nādā wa huwa makẓūm* — of Jonah).

**And the picture of the word is worth noting:** *nidāʾ* is **a call made from a distance.** One does not make *nidāʾ* to someone near; with him one makes *najwā* (a whisper).

**And this ties to another place in the sequence — this is a textual datum:**

| Prophet | Verb | Verse |
|---|---|---|
| **Noah** | *nādānā* — **he called upon Us** | 75 |
| **Abraham** | *wa **nādaynāhu*** — **We called upon him** | 104 |

**The same verb, the opposite direction.** Noah calls, Abraham is called. **I record this as an observation.**

**And what he said is not stated.** Elsewhere the Qurʾān states it: *fa-daʿā rabbahu **annī maghlūbun fa'ntaṣir*** (al-Qamar 54/10) — **treated in `054-kamer.md`.** Here the content is not given; only the act and its answer.

### فَلَنِعْمَ ٱلْمُجِيبُونَ — how excellent are the answerers

***Niʿma* — in Arabic the verb of praise (*fiʿl al-madḥ*).** Its opposite is *biʾsa* (how evil). **The mould is used to praise a thing in the highest degree.**

**A point of word order:** *fa-**la**-niʿma* — with *fāʾ* and *lām* at its head. **The grammarians count this *lām* as the answer to an elided oath.** At the head of the sentence, that is, there is an unstated oath: "By God, how excellent are We as answerers."

***Mujīb* — root ج-و-ب: to answer, to respond.** The concrete sense of the root is **"to cut through, to cleave"**: *jāba'l-arḍa* — he cleft the ground, he traversed it. In the Qurʾān: *wa Thamūda'lladhīna **jābū**'ṣ-ṣakhra bi'l-wād* (al-Fajr 89/9) — "and Thamūd, who hollowed the rocks in the valley" — **treated in `089-fecr.md`.**

**The link the lexicographers make:** an answer is what **cleaves** the obstacle standing before a question. The word goes out and its response comes back. **I give this link as a transmission from the lexicographers.**

**And the plural is worth noting:** *al-mujībūn* — "the answerers". **A plural of majesty.** The same form will be repeated in the sūra: *innā*, *jaʿalnā*, *taraknā*.

### مِنَ ٱلْكَرْبِ ٱلْعَظِيمِ — the great distress

***Karb* — root ك-ر-ب.** The original sense the lexicographers give: **a distress that narrows a person's breast and cuts off his breath.** *Kurba* — a trouble; *makrūb* — one in distress. **From the same root *karaba* also means "it drew near"**; the lexicographers make the link thus: distress is what draws near and clings to a person.

**And the construction occurs twice in the sūra — this is a countable fact:**

| Verse | Who | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **76** | **Noah** and his household | *wa najjaynāhu wa ahlahu mina'l-karbi'l-ʿaẓīm* |
| **115** | **Moses, Aaron** and their people | *wa najjaynāhumā wa qawmahumā mina'l-karbi'l-ʿaẓīm* |

**The same construction, the same verb (*najjaynā*), two different deliverances.** And both are deliverances **from water**: the one from the flood, the other from the sea. **Whether the text sets up this link is not said; the sharing of the words is a datum.**

***Najjaynā* — root ن-ج-و, Form II: to save.** The concrete sense of the root is **"high ground"**: *najwa* — a rise one climbs to be safe from a flood. **"To save", then, is really "to raise up high".** And in the context of the flood this picture works directly.

### وَجَعَلْنَا ذُرِّيَّتَهُۥ هُمُ ٱلْبَاقِينَ — the ones who remained

***Dhurriyya* — root ذ-ر-ر or ذ-ر-و.** Two derivations are transmitted: **(a) from ذ-ر-ر — a mote, a small particle; (b) from ذ-ر-و — to scatter, to spread.** Both arrive at the picture of "small particles spread abroad". **I issue no firm etymological ruling.**

***Al-bāqīn* — root ب-ق-ي: to remain, to be left over.**

**A point of word order:** *jaʿalnā dhurriyyatahu **hum** al-bāqīn* — the pronoun *hum* comes between. **This is the *ḍamīr al-faṣl* and it sets up *ḥaṣr* (restriction):** "the ones who remain are **only** his progeny".

**And this is information the text gives; its detail is not given.** In the classical commentaries it is said, on the basis of this verse, that the whole of humanity comes from Noah's line. **The verse does not say this openly; it says "the ones who remained are his progeny". I record this much and write no more.**

### وَتَرَكْنَا عَلَيْهِ فِى ٱلْءَاخِرِينَ — and the first form of the mould

**This sentence occurs four times in the sūra: 78 (Noah), 108 (Abraham), 119 (Moses-Aaron — *ʿalayhimā*), 129 (Elijah). It was given in a table in the section on the structure.**

**And there is an *ellipsis* in the sentence:** *taraknā ʿalayhi* — "We left upon him". **What was left is not said.** The verb has no object.

**The completions the grammarians record:**

| Completion | Sense |
|---|---|
| *thanāʾan ḥasanan* — a good remembrance | "We left him a good name among those who came after" |
| *dhikran* — a memory | "We left a memory of him" |
| **The next verse itself** | What was left is verse 79: the sentence ***salāmun ʿalā Nūḥ*** |

**The third is worth noting and is the closest to the text:** the seventy-ninth verse is directly a **sentence of peace** and stands like a quoted utterance. **What "We left", then, may be that very sentence — the word that remained on people's tongues.**

**I record this as a view and impose no preference;** but that all three moulds (78-79, 108-109, 129-130) come in the same order gives this reading a ground.

***Al-ākhirīn* — root أ-خ-ر: to come after.** "Those who come after." **And note: in the seventy-ninth verse *al-ʿālamīn* (the worlds) occurs, and in the eighty-second *al-ākharīn* (the others) will occur.** The three words resemble one another but are three distinct things:

| Word | Root | Sense | Verse |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ٱلْءَاخِرِين** | أ-خ-ر | Those who come after | 78 |
| **ٱلْعَٰلَمِين** | ع-ل-م | The worlds | 79 |
| **ٱلْءَاخَرِين** | أ-خ-ر | **The others** (the rest) | 82 |
