*Wa laqad sabaqat kalimatunā li-ʿibādina'l-mursalīn · Innahum la-humu'l-manṣūrūn · Wa inna jundanā la-humu'l-ghālibūn · Fa-tawalla ʿanhum ḥattā ḥīn · Wa abṣirhum fa-sawfa yubṣirūn · A-fa-bi-ʿadhābinā yastaʿjilūn · Fa-idhā nazala bi-sāḥatihim fa-sāʾa ṣabāḥu'l-mundharīn · Wa tawalla ʿanhum ḥattā ḥīn · Wa abṣir fa-sawfa yubṣirūn*

"And Our word has gone forth already concerning Our servants the messengers: they will surely be helped, and Our host will surely prevail. So turn away from them for a time, and watch them — soon they will see. Do they seek to hasten Our punishment? But when it comes down into their courtyard, how evil will be the morning of those who were warned! Turn away from them for a time, and watch — soon they will see."

### وَلَقَدْ سَبَقَتْ كَلِمَتُنَا — a word gone forth already

***Sabaqa* — root س-ب-ق: to go ahead, to be first.** The root was treated at `056-vakia.md` 56/10, 60 (*al-sābiqūn*, *masbūqīn*).

**And the choice of the verb is worth noting — as my own reading:** the verse does not say "We have a word", it says **"Our word went ahead"**. **The promise, that is, stands in front of the events** — not a consolation given afterwards.

***Kalima* — root ك-ل-م.** **And in Arabic *kalima* means not a single word but also a saying, a decree.** In the thirty-first verse the same idea occurred with another word: *fa-ḥaqqa ʿalaynā **qawlu** rabbinā* — "the word of our Lord has come true against us".

| Verse | Word | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **31** | *qawlu rabbinā* | **Those in the punishment** — against them |
| **171** | *kalimatunā* | **The messengers** — in their favour |

**The same idea, two words, two opposite directions. This is a textual datum.**

***Li-ʿibādina'l-mursalīn* — "Our servants the messengers".** **And the construct uses the same word as the closing mould of the sequence:** *min **ʿibādinā**'l-muʾminīn* (81, 111, 132). **The same structure: *ʿibādinā* + an attribute.**

| Verse | Construct | Attribute |
|---|---|---|
| **81, 111, 132** | *min ʿibādi**nā*** | *al-muʾminīn* |
| **171** | *li-ʿibādi**nā*** | *al-mursalīn* |

**This is a datum about a word that binds the sequence's closing mould to the sūra's closing block.**

### إِنَّهُمْ لَهُمُ ٱلْمَنصُورُونَ · وَإِنَّ جُندَنَا لَهُمُ ٱلْغَٰلِبُونَ — two promises

**And the two verses are in the same mould: *inna* + a noun + *la-humu* + a definite active or passive participle.**

| Verse | Who | Word | Pattern |
|---|---|---|---|
| **172** | *innahum* — the messengers | *al-**manṣūrūn*** | **Passive participle** — those who **are helped** |
| **173** | *jundanā* — Our host | *al-**ghālibūn*** | **Active participle** — those who **prevail** |

**The difference of pattern is worth noting — as my own reading:** the messengers are named with the **passive** pattern (those helped), the host with the **active** (those who prevail). **Help comes to the messenger, that is; the victory belongs to the host.** The two sentences say the same outcome from two different directions.

***Manṣūr* — root ن-ص-ر.** **And in the hundred and sixteenth verse it was said of Moses and Aaron *wa naṣarnāhum*. The same root, the one realised in the past, the other a promise given for the future.**

***Al-ghālibūn* — root غ-ل-ب.** **And in the hundred and sixteenth verse it was said *fa-kānū humu'l-ghālibīn*. The table was given in the section on the hundred and sixteenth verse.**

**Verses one hundred and seventy-two and one hundred and seventy-three, that is, turn into a general rule two things that were realised in the past in the Moses-Aaron section (help and victory). This is a link that can be verified from the text.**

***Jund* — a soldier, an army, a company.** **And the construct is *junda**nā*** — "Our host". Whose it is is not said, of whom it consists is not said. The verse does not give it, and neither do I.**

### فَتَوَلَّ عَنْهُمْ حَتَّىٰ حِينٍ — and a command repeated twice

**And here is the plainest repetition of the sūra. Verses one hundred and seventy-four to one hundred and seventy-five and verses one hundred and seventy-eight to one hundred and seventy-nine are almost exactly the same:**

| | **174-175** | **178-179** | Difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| First verse | ***fa**-tawalla ʿanhum ḥattā ḥīn* | ***wa**-tawalla ʿanhum ḥattā ḥīn* | **Conjunction:** *fāʾ* / *wāw* |
| Second verse | *wa abṣir**hum** fa-sawfa yubṣirūn* | *wa abṣir fa-sawfa yubṣirūn* | **Object:** *hum* present / absent |

**A difference of two words. This is a fact that can be verified from the text.**

**And there are two verses in between (176-177) — the repetition, then, is not idle; it sets up a frame:**

| Position | Content |
|---|---|
| **174-175** | Turn away + watch |
| **176-177** | **Do they seek to hasten it? — how evil that morning is** |
| **178-179** | Turn away + watch |

**A warning has been set between the two commands, that is. This is a widespread structure in the Qurʾān, and in `056-vakia.md` a note was entered for the same structure (the repetition of verse 74 = 96): *the repetition draws a block boundary*.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the *abṣir**hum*** ("watch **them**") of the first command has an object; in the second the object has dropped: *abṣir* ("watch"). **In the second command, that is, what is to be watched is not specified.** The first says to look at them, the second only to look. **This is an observation about the structure; the grammarians issue no firm ruling on a difference of sense.**

***Tawalla* — root و-ل-ي, Form V: to turn away.** **And the verb had occurred in the ninetieth verse:** *fa-**tawallaw** ʿanhu mudbirīn* — **Abraham's people had turned away from him.**

| Verse | Verb | Who from whom |
|---|---|---|
| **90** | *fa-**tawallaw** ʿanhu* | **The people, from Abraham** |
| **174, 178** | *fa-**tawalla** ʿanhum* | **The Prophet, from them** |

**The same verb, the same preposition (*ʿan*), the opposite direction. This is a fact that can be verified from the text.**

**And at `051-zariyat.md` 51/54 the same command was treated:** *fa-tawalla ʿanhum fa-mā anta bi-malūm.* **I rely on that treatment.** **The difference here: in al-Dhāriyāt a consolation follows the command ("you are not to be blamed"); in al-Ṣāffāt a term is given ("for a time").**

***Ḥattā ḥīn* — "for a time".** **And *ḥīn* for the third time in the sūra** (148, 174, 178). **The table was given in the section on the hundred and forty-eighth verse.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the command is not an abandonment but a **postponement**. *Ḥattā* (until) announces a limit. **And the term is left indefinite** (*ḥīn* is indefinite). **Not "leave them", then, but "leave them for now".**

### وَأَبْصِرْهُمْ فَسَوْفَ يُبْصِرُونَ — watch

***Abṣara* — root ب-ص-ر, Form IV: to see, to watch.** *Baṣar* — the eye, sight; *baṣīra* — inward sight. **The root was treated at `051-zariyat.md` 51/21 (*a-fa-lā tubṣirūn*) and in `075-kiyame.md`.**

**And a point of word order is worth noting: the same root is used both in the command and in the threat.**

- *abṣir* — an **imperative**, to the Prophet.
- *yubṣirūn* — an **imperfect**, about them.

**That is: "you watch — and they too will see."** **Both sides will do the same act; at different times.**

**And a comparison with the hundred and seventieth verse:**

| Verse | The threat | Verb |
|---|---|---|
| **170** | *fa-sawfa **yaʿlamūn*** | **They will know** |
| **175, 179** | *fa-sawfa **yubṣirūn*** | **They will see** |

**The same mould (*fa-sawfa* + imperfect), three times, two different verbs.** **The sūra's last block sets knowing and seeing side by side.** This is a countable fact.

### أَفَبِعَذَابِنَا يَسْتَعْجِلُونَ — do they seek to hasten?

***Istaʿjala* — root ع-ج-ل, Form X: to ask that a thing come quickly.** *ʿAjala* — to want a thing before its time. **The root was treated at `051-zariyat.md` 51/14, 59 (*tastaʿjilūn*, *fa-lā yastaʿjilūn*) and in `042-sura.md`.**

**A point of word order:** *a-fa-**bi-ʿadhābinā** yastaʿjilūn* — the prepositional phrase has been brought forward. **The ordinary order would have been *a-fa-yastaʿjilūna bi-ʿadhābinā*.** **The bringing forward creates emphasis: "is it Our punishment they seek to hasten?"**

**I record this as my own reading:** the question is a question of astonishment. **To ask for a thing no one would think of asking for** — and this accords with the bearing at the start of the sūra: in the twelfth verse they were mocking, in the fifteenth they were saying "sorcery". **The mockery turns at the end into a challenge.**

### فَإِذَا نَزَلَ بِسَاحَتِهِمْ — when it comes down into their courtyard

**And this verse is one of the most concrete images in the sūra.**

***Nazala* — root ن-ز-ل: to come down, to alight.** **And the use of the word in this context is important: *nazala bi-* in Arabic means "to alight in a place, to encamp there"** — the arriving and alighting of a traveller.

**And the word *nuzul* analysed in `056-vakia.md` is from this root:** the first hospitality set out for a guest on his arrival. **In the sixty-second verse *khayrun nuzulan* was said.**

**The sūra uses the same root twice, then, and in both there is an idea of *alighting*:**

| Verse | Word | Who comes |
|---|---|---|
| **62** | *nuzulan* | **The hospitality set out for a guest** |
| **177** | *nazala bi-sāḥatihim* | **The punishment alights in their courtyard** |

**This is a fact read off the choice of words.**

***Sāḥa* — root س-و-ح: the open space before a house or a settlement, a courtyard, a square.**

**And the word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the choice of word announces a **distance.** The punishment does not come down "upon them"; it comes down **into their courtyard** — that is, before the door. **This is the picture of an army encamping before a settlement**, and in Arabic this is expressed exactly as *nazala bi-sāḥatihim*. **The next word completes it: *ṣabāḥ*.**

### فَسَآءَ صَبَاحُ ٱلْمُنذَرِينَ — how evil that morning is

***Sāʾa* — in Arabic the verb of dispraise (*fiʿl al-dhamm*).** Its opposite is *niʿma*. **And in the seventy-fifth verse *fa-la-**niʿma**'l-mujībūn* had occurred.**

| Verse | Verb | Of what |
|---|---|---|
| **75** | *fa-la-**niʿma**'l-mujībūn* | **Praise** — the answerers |
| **177** | *fa-**sāʾa** ṣabāḥu'l-mundharīn* | **Dispraise** — the morning of those warned |

**The same kind of verb (praise / dispraise), at the two ends of the sūra. This is a textual datum.**

***Ṣabāḥ* — root ص-ب-ح.** **And the use of the word here is connected with the language of war in Arabic, and this is worth noting:** among the Arabs raids were most often made at dawn, and *ṣabbaḥa'l-qawma* meant "he made a dawn raid upon the people". **For this reason the word *ṣabāḥ* in this context carries the suggestion of "the hour of the raid".**

**And at `054-kamer.md` 54/38 the same root occurred in the same context:** *wa laqad **ṣabbaḥa**hum bukratan ʿadhābun mustaqirr.* **And in the hundred and thirty-seventh verse *muṣbiḥīn* was said of the ruin of the people of Lot.**

**Three occurrences, the same root, the same hour:**

| Place | Expression | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/137** | *la-tamurrūna ʿalayhim **muṣbiḥīn*** | Passing by the ruin in the **morning** |
| **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/177** | *fa-sāʾa **ṣabāḥu**'l-mundharīn* | The **morning** on which the punishment comes |
| **Al-Qamar 54/38** | *wa laqad **ṣabbaḥa**hum bukratan* | The punishment that came to the people of Lot in the **morning** |

**This is a countable fact about a word.**

***Al-mundharīn* — root ن-ذ-ر, a passive participle: those warned.** **And the word for the second time in the sūra** (73, 177). **In the section on the seventy-third verse the difference *mundhirīn / mundharīn* was given in a table.**

**I record this as my own reading:** in the seventy-third verse it was said *"look how the end of those warned turned out"*, and thereafter the accounts of six prophets were recounted. **In the hundred and seventy-seventh the same word returns, and this time it is used of the addressee himself.** **The sūra's sequence of prophets is thus set between two occurrences of the word *mundharīn*.** This is a frame that can be verified from the text.

---

## 37/180-182 — سُبْحَٰنَ رَبِّكَ رَبِّ ٱلْعِزَّةِ عَمَّا يَصِفُونَ · وَسَلَٰمٌ عَلَى ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ · وَٱلْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ رَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ

*Subḥāna rabbika rabbi'l-ʿizzati ʿammā yaṣifūn · Wa salāmun ʿala'l-mursalīn · Wa'l-ḥamdu li'llāhi rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*

"Glory be to your Lord, the Lord of might, above what they describe. And peace be upon the messengers who were sent. And praise be to God, the Lord of all the worlds."

### Three verses, three pieces of work

**The sūra closes with three short sentences, and the three do three different pieces of work:**

| Verse | Work | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| **180** | **Declaring free** — holding away from what is ascribed | **Upward** |
| **181** | **Peace** — to the messengers | **Sideways** |
| **182** | **Praise** | **Upward** |

**And the middle verse is a salutation set between two sentences of declaring free and of praise. This is a fact of word order.**

### سُبْحَٰنَ رَبِّكَ رَبِّ ٱلْعِزَّةِ عَمَّا يَصِفُونَ — an expanded repetition

**The verse is the expanded form of the hundred and fifty-ninth. The table was given in the section on the hundred and fifty-ninth verse.**

**Two things added:**

1. ***Rabbika*** — "**your** Lord". **The address turns to the Prophet.** In the hundred and fifty-ninth verse there was the divine name (*Allāh*).
2. ***Rabbi'l-ʿizzati*** — "the Lord of might". **An attributive construct is added.**

***ʿIzza* — root ع-ز-ز.** **And the concrete sense of the root is worth noting: *arḍu'l-ʿazāz* — hard, barren ground that gives no passage.** *ʿAzza* — he became strong, he prevailed, he became unattainable. **In the root there are two things: *hardness* and *unattainability*.**

**The root was treated in `048-fetih.md`, `059-hasr.md`, `063-munafikun.md` and `053-necm.md`.**

**And the placing of the construct here is worth noting — as my own reading:** *waṣf* (describing) is to draw the bounds of a thing. ***ʿIzza* is unattainability.** **The two words meet in the same sentence:** those who describe draw a bound, the one described is unattainable. **The sentence is built on this tension.**

**And at `063-munafikun.md` 63/8 the same word had occurred as a claim** (*la-yukhrijanna'l-aʿazzu minha'l-adhall*) — there might was supposed to be something shared out among human beings. **Here it is attributed to a single place.**

### وَسَلَٰمٌ عَلَى ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ — and the closing of the sequence

**And this verse completes the table set up in the section on the structure of the sūra.**

**The four salutations of the sequence had been given to individual persons:**

| Verse | The peace | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| **79** | *salāmun ʿalā **Nūḥin** fi'l-ʿālamīn* | One person |
| **109** | *salāmun ʿalā **Ibrāhīm*** | One person |
| **120** | *salāmun ʿalā **Mūsā wa Hārūn*** | Two persons |
| **130** | *salāmun ʿalā **Il Yāsīn*** | One person |
| **181** | *wa salāmun ʿalā **al-mursalīn*** | **All of them** |

**And what was recorded in the section on the structure is completed here:** Lot and Jonah received no peace in the sequence, **but both had been presented as *mina'l-mursalīn*** (133, 139). **The hundred and eighty-first verse says *al-mursalīn*.**

**This is a link of wording that can be verified from the text. And whether the link is made is not said in the verse — I recorded this in the section on the structure as well.**

***Al-mursalīn* — for the sixth and last time in the sūra** (37, 123, 133, 139, 171, 181).

**And a difference of word order must be recorded:**

| | **The salutations in the sequence** | **181** |
|---|---|---|
| A *wāw* at the head | **Absent** | **Present** — *wa salāmun* |
| The addressee | **A definite name** | **A definite plural** |

**The *wāw* at the head binds the verse to the preceding verse.** **The peace, that is, comes not as an independent sentence but as the continuation of the sentence of declaring free.** And the hundred and eighty-second verse is joined with a *wāw* too. **The three verses are a single chain.**

### وَٱلْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ رَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ — and the link with al-Fātiḥa

**And the sūra closes with the first verse of al-Fātiḥa.**

**The construction *al-ḥamdu li'llāhi rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* was analysed in detail at `001-fatiha.md` 1/1** — the differences between *ḥamd*, *shukr* and *madḥ*, the work of the *lām*, the root of the word *rabb* and the plural form of *ʿālamīn* were given there. **I do not repeat it and I rely on that treatment.**

**What is to be added here is the link within the sūra — and this is a textual datum:**

| Verse | Construction | Who says it |
|---|---|---|
| **87** | *fa-mā ẓannukum bi-**rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*** | **Abraham** |
| **182** | *wa'l-ḥamdu li'llāhi **rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*** | **The closing** |

**The sūra closes with the construction it set in Abraham's mouth.** **And Abraham had asked a question there; the sūra gives a judgement here.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the question asked ninety-five verses earlier (*"what do you suppose of the Lord of all the worlds?"*) was left unanswered. **The sūra's last verse gives its own answer to that question: *al-ḥamdu li'llāh*.** The text does not set up this link openly; the sharing of the words is a datum.

### And the closing itself

**That a sūra of the Qurʾān should close with *al-ḥamdu li'llāh* is rare.** **And al-Ṣāffāt's closing is not of three words but of three verses:** declaring free, peace, praise.

**I record this as my own reading:** the three sentences close the sūra's three main subjects.

| Sentence | The subject it closes |
|---|---|
| ***Subḥāna rabbika…*** | **The subject of block 149-170** — what is ascribed to God |
| ***Wa salāmun ʿala'l-mursalīn*** | **The subject of block 75-148** — the sequence of prophets |
| ***Wa'l-ḥamdu li'llāh…*** | **The subject of block 1-11** — God's oneness and lordship |

**The closing, that is, gathers the sūra in reverse order: the subject treated last comes first, the subject treated first comes last.** **This is an observation; the text claims no such order.**

### What this says to the present

Through a hundred and eighty-two verses the sūra carried on a dispute: who serves what, who fabricates what, who leads whom astray, who witnessed what. **And in its closing there is not one sentence of dispute.**

**What is worth recording is this:** the text gives its last word not as a proof but as a **bearing** — to hold away, to give peace, to praise. **In the section on al-Wāqiʿa it was recorded that the sūra's last command was not a piece of knowledge or an act but a bearing** (`056-vakia.md` 56/96). **Al-Ṣāffāt does the same thing three times over.**

And the middle sentence — *wa salāmun ʿala'l-mursalīn* — is a human utterance: **peace is something said among people.** The sūra sets a salutation between two sentences of declaring free. **Between two sentences that look upward, that is, there is a sentence that looks sideways.**

---

## The sūra as a whole — looking back

### Structure

| Block | Verses | Work | Closing |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | 1-11 | Three oaths, the ruling of God's oneness, the guarding of the sky, the proof from clay | *min ṭīnin lāzib* |
| **II** | 12-39 | Mockery, the objection to resurrection, the reckoning and the quarrel in the fire | *mā kuntum taʿmalūn* |
| **III** | 40-61 | The picture of the garden and the scene of the *qarīn* | *fa'l-yaʿmali'l-ʿāmilūn* |
| **IV** | 62-74 | *Zaqqūm*, following the forefathers, the end of those warned | *illā ʿibāda'llāhi'l-mukhlaṣīn* |
| **V** | 75-148 | **The sequence of prophets** — six sections | *fa-mattaʿnāhum ilā ḥīn* |
| **VI** | 149-170 | The dispute over daughters and sons, the angels, "we are the ones who stand in ranks" | *fa-sawfa yaʿlamūn* |
| **VII** | 171-182 | The promise given to the messengers, "turn away" twice, the closing | *rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* |

### The sequence of prophets and the closing moulds

**The full table was given in the section `The backbone of the sūra` above. Its summary:**

| Prophet | *taraknā* | *salām* | *najzi'l-muḥsinīn* | *min ʿibādinā* |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Noah** (75-82) | 78 | 79 | 80 | 81 |
| **Abraham** (83-113) | 108 | 109 | **105 and 110** | 111 |
| **Moses-Aaron** (114-122) | 119 | 120 | 121 | 122 |
| **Elijah** (123-132) | 129 | 130 | 131 | 132 |
| **Lot** (133-138) | — | — | — | — |
| **Jonah** (139-148) | — | — | — | — |

**And the two missing salutations are given collectively in verse 181: *wa salāmun ʿala'l-mursalīn* — and both had been presented as *mina'l-mursalīn* (133, 139).**

### Repetitions verifiable within the sūra

**All are facts countable from the text; they are not interpretation.**

| Repetition | Where | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **A repeated sentence** | *fa/wa-aqbala baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍin yatasāʾalūn* — **27 and 50** | The fire and the garden, the same sentence |
| **A repeated sentence** | *a-idhā mitnā wa kunnā turāban wa ʿiẓāman* — **16 and 53** | *mabʿūthūn* / *madīnūn* — one word of difference |
| **A repeated sentence** | *fa/wa-tawalla ʿanhum ḥattā ḥīn · wa abṣir(hum) fa-sawfa yubṣirūn* — **174-175 and 178-179** | A difference of two words |
| **A refrain** | *illā ʿibāda'llāhi'l-mukhlaṣīn* — **40, 74, 128, 160**; and in **169** in the mouths of the deniers | The condition of deliverance, at the end inside a falsehood |
| **A mould** | *innā kadhālika najzi'l-muḥsinīn* — **80, 105, 121, 131**; *kadhālika* — **110** | *Innā* absent only at 110 |
| **A mould** | *innā kadhālika **nafʿalu bi'l-mujrimīn*** — **34** | The reverse of the closing mould |
| **A command** | *fa'staftihim* — **11 and 149** | Two block openings |
| **A mould** | *inna hādhā la-huwa …* — **60** (*al-fawzu'l-ʿaẓīm*) and **106** (*al-balāʾu'l-mubīn*) | A ruling with three reinforcements |
| **A mould** | *wa innā la-naḥnu …* — **165, 166** | The same threefold reinforcement |
| **Declaring free** | *subḥāna … ʿammā yaṣifūn* — **159 and 180** | The second expanded |
| **A mould** | *fa-sawfa yaʿlamūn* (**170**), *fa-sawfa yubṣirūn* (**175, 179**) | To know / to see |
| **A construction** | *al-karbi'l-ʿaẓīm* — **76 (Noah) and 115 (Moses-Aaron)** | Two deliverances from water |
| **A word** | *saqīm* — **89 (Abraham) and 145 (Jonah)** | The same attribute, two prophets |
| **A word** | *mulīm* — **142**; and `051-zariyat.md` 51/40 (Pharaoh) | The same attribute, opposite outcomes |
| **A mould** | *thumma aghraqna'l-ākharīn* (**82**) / *thumma dammarna'l-ākharīn* (**136**) | The same structure, two sections |

### The verse-ending

| Verses | Sound | How many verses |
|---|---|---|
| 1-3 | Nunated **-ā** | 3 |
| 4-11 | Long vowel + **b / d / q** | 8 |
| 12-182 | Long vowel + **n** or **m** | **171 — without exception** |

**This is a count; no reckoning or claim of a secret is set up.**

### The limits the sūra keeps

**The following are limits recorded per the method (`STYLE.md`) and read off the text itself:**

| Verse | What it does |
|---|---|
| **71** | *aktharu'l-awwalīn* — **"most", not all** |
| **113** | *muḥsinun wa ẓālimun li-nafsihi* — **two kinds of human being from the same line** |
| **126** | *rabba ābāʾikumu'l-awwalīn* — **the Lord of the forefathers too; no wholesale condemnation** |
| **128** | *illā ʿibāda'llāhi'l-mukhlaṣīn* — **an exception from within the people who denied** |
| **148** | *fa-āmanū* — **a people who believe at the end of the sequence** |

**All five run in the same direction:** the judgement is tied not to a group but to an **attribute**. **This is the counterpart within the sūra of the principle recorded in `STYLE.md`, "no wholesale judgement is built".**

### Disagreements where no preference is adopted in this commentary

|---|---|
| **1-3** | Who or what is sworn by — angels, the ranks of prayer, birds, a mixture |
| **5** | Why *al-mashāriq* alone (without the settings being named) |
| **6** | *Bi-zīnatini'l-kawākib* — a construct or an appositive |
| **8** | *Lā yassammaʿūn* / *lā yasmaʿūn* |
| **11** | Who *man khalaqnā* are — angels, the heavens and earth, the peoples of the past |
| **12** | *ʿAjibta* / *ʿajibtu* — and the attribution of the verb *ʿajab* |
| **22** | *Azwājahum* — their spouses, their like, or their *qarīn*s |
| **28** | *ʿAni'l-yamīn* — the side of religion, force, oaths, the trusted side |
| **28-32** | Who the speaking parties are — followers/leaders, or human beings/satans |
| **41** | *Rizqun maʿlūm* — "known" in what respect |
| **47** | *Yunzafūn* / *yanzifūn*; which harm *ghawl* removes |
| **49** | *Bayḍun maknūn* — colour, being preserved, or smoothness |
| **51** | *Qarīn* — a human being or another kind of being |
| **58-59** | Who says these two verses |
| **61** | *Li-mithli hādhā* — is *mithl* intensification or real likeness |
| **63** | *Fitna* — an occasion of dispute, a punishment, or both |
| **65** | *Ruʾūsu'sh-shayāṭīn* — how the comparison works |
| **88-89** | *Fa-naẓara … innī saqīm* — five different explanations |
| **93** | *Bi'l-yamīn* — the right hand, force, an oath |
| **96** | *Wa mā taʿmalūn* — is *mā* relative or verbal-noun |
| **99-113** | **Which son is the sacrifice** — Ishmael / Isaac |
| **102** | *Mādhā tarā* / *mādhā turā*; the character of the consultation |
| **103** | Whether the apodosis of *lammā* is elided |
| **107** | *Dhibḥin ʿaẓīm* — "great" in what respect |
| **110** | Why *innā* is absent |
| **125** | *Baʿl* — the name of an idol, "master", or a foreign name |
| **130** | *Il Yāsīn* / *Ilyāsīn* / *Āl Yāsīn* |

| **160** | Whether the exception attaches to 158 or to 159 |

### Points in this file of which I could not be certain

**I record them plainly:**

- **The reason why the Lot and Jonah sections do not receive the closing mould.** The fact is certain; four explanations were given for the reason and none can be proved from the text.
- **The reason for the absence of *innā* in verse 110.** The fact is certain; three explanations were given.
- **The link between the subject of the oath in the first verse and the speaking party in verse 165.** The sharing of the root is a datum; that the link is made by the text cannot be shown.
- **Who the son to be sacrificed is.** The verse gives no name; the table of disagreement was given and no preference was imposed.
- **The attribution of the verb *ʿajab* on the second reading in verse 12.** The theological dispute was not entered; two approaches were recorded.
- **Who is speaking in verses 164-166.** The context runs toward the angels, but the text gives no verb (*qālū*).
